Two different nodal families may give the same family of cycles.
This already happens for nodal curves over the field
k
,
and it is one of the main difficulties we have to deal with in the following.

Notation 3.6
.

Given a nodal family of one-cycles
S\leftarrow\Gamma_{S}\to X,\Gamma_{S}=\sum m_{i}Z_{i}
, and a morphism
f:T\to S
, we denote by
\Gamma_{T}=f^{*}\Gamma_{S}\to T
, the pull-back nodal family over
T
, where
f^{*}\Gamma_{S}=\sum m_{i}(Z_{i}\times_{S}T)
.
If
f
is a locally closed immersion, we also use the notation
\Gamma_{T}=\Gamma_{S}|_{T}
to denote the pull-back nodal family.

Remark 3.7
.

Given a nodal family of one-cycles
S\leftarrow\Gamma_{S}\to X
and a morphism
f:T\to S
, by
[
SV00b
, Lemma 3.3.10]
and the definition of associated families of cycles, we have
[f^{*}\Gamma_{S}]=f^{*}[\Gamma_{S}]
as equi-dimensional families of one-cycles over
T
.

Definition 3.8

(relative projective extension of a nodal family)
.

Let
f_{T}:T\to S
be a morphism between quasi-projective algebraic sets over a field
k
,
and let
f_{V}:V\to S
be a generically finite projective surjective
S
-morphism from a quasi-projective
k
-algebraic set
V
.
Assume that there is a dense open subset
V^{0}\subset V
which admits a
k
-morphism
g:V^{0}\to T
.
Given a nodal family
T\leftarrow\Gamma_{T}=\sum_{i}m_{i}Z_{i}\to X
,
we define a
projective extension to
f_{V}:V\to S
(relative to
S
)
, or just
projective extension to
V
if
S=\text{Spec }k
, of the family
\Gamma_{T}
to be a nodal family of one-cycles
V\leftarrow\Gamma_{V}\to X
such that
\Gamma_{V}|_{V^{0}}=g^{*}\Gamma_{T}
.

If
T
is pointed, we require that
V^{0}
is pointed, and that
g:V^{0}\to T
is a morphism of pointed schemes.

By semi-stable reduction
[
dJ96
, Theorem 5.8]
, given
f_{T}:T\to S
and a nodal family
T\leftarrow\Gamma_{T}\to X
, there always exists a projective extension (relative to
S
) of
\Gamma_{T}
to some
f_{V}:V\to S
.

Now we describe some of the basic structures of the space of one-cycles.

Proposition 3.9
.

Let
S_{1},S_{2}
be two connected algebraic sets and
\Gamma_{1}\subset S_{1}\times X,\Gamma_{2}\subset S_{2}\times X
be two equi-dimensional families of
r
-cycles in
X
.
Assume that the cycles in the two families are algebraically equivalent (Definition
1.10
).
There is a connected algebraic set
S
and an equi-dimensional family of
r
-cycles
\Gamma\subset S\times X
, and morphisms
f_{1}:S_{1}\to S,f_{2}:S_{2}\to S
such that
\Gamma_{1}=f_{1}^{*}\Gamma,\Gamma_{2}=f_{2}^{*}\Gamma
. Moreover, if both
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are normal/smooth/projective, we may choose
S
to be normal/smooth/projective.

Proof.

Take two points
s_{1}\in S_{2},s_{2}\in S_{2}
. Denote by
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
the cycle over
s_{1},s_{2}
.
By assumption,
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
are algebraically equivalent.
Thus there is a smooth projective curve
S_{3}
, two points
a,b\in S_{3}
and a family of
r
-cycles
\Gamma_{3}\subset S_{3}\times X
such that the cycle over
a,b
are
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
.
This follows from the definition of algebraic equivalence. Indeed, by definition of algebraic equivalence, we may find a cycle
\Delta
and a family of cycles
\Gamma_{T}
over a smooth projective curve
T
and two points
t_{1},t_{2}\in T
such that the cycle over
t_{1}
(resp.
t_{2}
) is
\gamma_{1}+\Delta
(resp.
\gamma_{2}+\Delta
) (
[
Ful98
, Example 10.3.2]
). Then we take
S_{3}
to be
T
and the family of cycles
\Gamma_{3}
to be
\Gamma_{T}-T\times\Delta
.

Define
S=S_{1}\times S_{2}\times S_{3}
, with
p_{i}:S\to S_{i},i=1,2,3,
the projections. Define
\Gamma=p_{1}^{*}\Gamma_{1}+p_{2}^{*}\Gamma_{2}-p_{3}^{*}\Gamma_{3}
.
Finally, define

f_{1}:S_{1}\to S,x\mapsto(x,s_{2},b)

and

f_{2}:S_{2}\to S,y\mapsto(s_{1},y,a).

It is straightforward to verify that these are the desired morphisms.

Since
S_{3}
is a smooth projective curve, if both
S_{1},S_{2}
are normal, or smooth, or projective, so is
S
.
∎

Now we can state the main technical results of this section.

Theorem 3.10
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety defined over an algebraically closed field
k
.
Let
(U,\Gamma_{U})
be an equi-dimensional family of one-cycles over an irreducible variety
U
and let
u_{0},u_{1}\in U
be two points in
U
such that

\Gamma_{U}|_{u_{0}}=\Gamma_{U}|_{u_{1}}=\gamma

as cycles.
Then there is

(1)

a nodal family of one-cycles
V\leftarrow\Gamma_{V}\to X
over a normal quasi-projective variety
V
, with a morphism
f:V\to U
, and a lifting
v_{0},v_{1}\in V
of the points
u_{0},u_{1}\in U
;

(2)

a nodal family of one-cycles
T\leftarrow\Gamma_{T}\to X
over a two pointed connected projective curve
(T,t_{0},t_{1})
, where
t_{0},t_{1}
are smooth points of
T
;

(3)

an open subset
U^{0}\subset U
such that for any chosen point
u
in
U^{0}
, for any pair of points
v,v^{\prime}\in f^{-1}(u)\subset V
, a nodal family of one-cycles

(T_{v,v^{\prime}},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})\leftarrow\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}\to X,

over a connected two pointed projective curve
(T_{v,v^{\prime}},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})
where
t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v}
,
t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}}
are smooth points of
T_{v,v^{\prime}}
;

(4)

after choosing a point
u\in U^{0}
, a nodal family of one-cycles
W\leftarrow\Gamma_{W}\to X
over a connected projective algebraic set
W
(depending on
u
),

such that

(i)

The morphism
f:V\to U
is generically finite, surjective and projective.

(ii)

f^{*}\Gamma_{U}=[\Gamma_{V}]
as equi-dimensional family of one-cycles over
V
.

(iii)

There is a morphism

\Psi:V\coprod T\coprod(\coprod_{v,v^{\prime}\in f^{-1}(u)}T_{v,v^{\prime}})\to W,

such that
\Gamma_{V}\coprod\Gamma_{T}\coprod(\coprod_{v,v^{\prime}}\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}})=\Psi^{*}\Gamma_{W}
.

(iv)

The morphism
\Psi
maps the pair of points
(v_{0},t_{0})
(resp.
(v_{1},t_{1})
,
(v,t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v})
,
(v^{\prime},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})
) to the same point in
W
.

(v)

The family of cycles over
[\Gamma_{T}]\to T
is constant. So necessarily, for each point
t
of
T
, the cycle class
[\Gamma_{T}|_{t}]
over
t
is
\gamma
.

(vi)

The family of cycles
[\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}]\to T_{v,v^{\prime}}
is constant. So necessarily, for each point
t\in T_{v,v^{\prime}}
, the cycle class
[\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}|_{t}]
over
t
is the same as the cycle
\Gamma_{U}|_{u}
.

Theorem 3.11
.

Keep the same assumptions as in Theorem
3.10
.
Also assume that
X
is separably rationally connected in codimension one.
Then there is

(1)

an equi-dimensional family of one-cycles
V\leftarrow\Gamma_{V}\to X
over a normal quasi-projective variety
V
, with a morphism
f:V\to U
, and a lifting
v_{0},v_{1}\in V
of the points
u_{0},u_{1}\in U
;

(2)

an equi-dimensional family of one-cycles
T\leftarrow\Gamma_{T}\to X
over a two pointed connected projective curve
(T,t_{0},t_{1})
, where
t_{0},t_{1}
are smooth points of
T
;

(3)

an open subset
U^{0}\subset U
such that for any chosen point
u
in
U^{0}
, for any pair of points
v,v^{\prime}
in the inverse image of
u
in
V
, an equi-dimensional family of one-cycles

(T_{v,v^{\prime}},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})\leftarrow\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}\to X,

over a connected two pointed projective curve
(T_{v,v^{\prime}},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})
, where
t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v}
,
t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}}
are smooth points of
T_{v,v^{\prime}}
;

(4)

after choosing a point
u\in U^{0}
, an equi-dimensional family of one-cycles
W\leftarrow\Gamma_{W}\to X
over a normal projective variety
W
(depending on
u\in U^{0}
),

such that

(i)

The morphism
f:V\to U
is generically finite, surjective and projective.

(ii)

f^{*}\Gamma_{U}=\Gamma_{V}
as equi-dimensional family of one-cycles over
V
.

(iii)

There is a morphism

\Psi:V\coprod T\coprod(\coprod_{v,v^{\prime}\in f^{-1}(u)}T_{v,v^{\prime}})\to W,

such that
\Gamma_{V}\coprod\Gamma_{T}\coprod(\coprod_{v,v^{\prime}}\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}})=\Psi^{*}\Gamma_{W}
.

(iv)

The morphism
\Psi
maps the pair of points
(v_{0},t_{0})
(resp.
(v_{1},t_{1})
,
(v,t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v})
,
(v^{\prime},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})
) to the same point in the smooth locus of
W
. It also maps
v_{0}
,
v_{1}
, and every singular point in
T,T_{v,v^{\prime}}
to the smooth locus of
W
.

(v)

The family of cycles
\Gamma_{T}\to T
is constant. So necessarily, for each point
t
of
T
,
\Gamma_{T}|_{t}=\gamma
.

(vi)

The family of cycles
\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}\to T_{v,v^{\prime}}
is constant. So necessarily, for each point
t\in T_{v,v^{\prime}}
,
\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}|_{t}=\Gamma_{U}|_{u}
.

In characteristic
0
, we may take
W
to be smooth and projective.

We first make some remarks and give an example in the following before giving the proof.

Remark 3.12
.

Theorem
3.11
is special to one-cycles on varieties that are SRC in codimension one.
Indeed, if the statements were true for a variety
X
and families of
r
-dimensional cycles, then the same argument as in Sections
4
(resp. Section
5
) would prove that

\tilde{N}^{d-r-1}H^{2d-2r-1}(X,\mathbb{Z})=N^{d-r-1}H^{2d-2r-1}(X,\mathbb{Z})

(resp. the corresponding equality of étale cohomology groups with
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-coefficients). But the examples of
[
BO21
]
show that this is not true in general.

Remark 3.13
.

Even if we start with a family of effective cycles, for the statements to be true, we have to use non-effective cycles.
In the proof, we have to add families of curves to the original family to achieve the desired deformation over the curve
T
.
Example
3.15
below shows that adding more curves is necessary.

Moreover, the statement is not a simple corollary of the existence of the universal family over the Chow variety (which exists only in characteristic
0
).
This is because we require that the family is parameterized by a normal variety, while the Chow variety is only semi-normal in
[
Kol96
]
by definition or satisfies no such normality condition at all in some other references such as
[
Fri91
]
.

It is possible that the morphism from the normalization of the Chow variety to the Chow variety maps two points to the same point.
If this happens, we take
U
to be the normalization of the Chow variety,
u,u^{\prime}
to be the two points mapping to the same point in the Chow variety, the existence of
V,T,W
in this case cannot be deduced from the existence of the universal family over the Chow variety.

Remark 3.14
.

Finally we remark that
U
being irreducible is not essential in the proof. But it simplifies the argument. If
U
is reducible and connected, one can use similar argument as in
[
KT23
, Section 8]
to find a connected algebraic set
V
. But in this case, we cannot choose
V
to admit a morphism to
U
. The best one can have is that for each irreducible component of
U
, there is an irreducible component of
V
with a projective, surjective morphism to this component.

Example 3.15
.

Take an irreducible nodal curve
C\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}
of degree
d
and arithmetic genus
g
.
We assume that
d>2g-1
.
Assume that there are
n
nodes for some
n>1
.
Let
C_{i}\to C
be the normalization at one of the nodes for
i=1,\ldots,n
. In this way, we get
n
morphisms from
n
different nodal curves (with
(n-1)
-nodes) to
\mathbb{P}^{3}
, whose image is
C
.
Call them
f_{i}:C_{i}\to\mathbb{P}^{3},i=1,\ldots,n
.

A degree
d
morphism from a curve of genus
g-1
to
\mathbb{P}^{3}
is determined by a choice of a line bundle of degree
d
and
4
sections without base point.
With our choice of
d,g
, any choice of
4
general sections works.
Using this, it is easy to show that there is an irreducible family of nodal curves with a morphism to
\mathbb{P}^{3}
over a smooth base
U
, a general member of which is a smooth curve of genus
g-1
, and the family contains all the morphisms
f_{i},i=1,\ldots,n,
above.

This gives a family of cycles over
U
as in Theorem
3.10
(choose
u_{0},u_{1}
to be any two of the points corresponding to the morphisms
f_{i},1\leq i\leq n
).
A general nodal deformation of the morphism
f_{i}
has to have a nodal curve of arithmetic genus
g-1
as the domain curve.
If the deformation fixes the image, it is of degree
1
as a morphism to
C
.
Thus the deformed morphisms to
C
(which is of arithmetic genus
g
) has to be the normalization at one of the nodes.
So the morphisms
f_{i}:C_{i}\to C,1\leq i\leq n,
are rigid.
As a result, any deformation between the
f_{i}
’s will have to deform the cycle class as well.

3.2.
Deforming complete intersection curves

This section is of auxiliary nature. We collect a few results that are essentially proved in
[
KT23
]
.
There are minor twists in the formulation.
So we provide details here.

Definition 3.16
.

Let
X
be a proper scheme over an algebraically closed field. A
deformation equivalence
(of curves mapping to
X
) is a diagram
(B,b_{1},b_{2})\leftarrow S\to X
, where

(1)

(B,b_{1},b_{2})
is a two pointed connected curve with smooth marked points
b_{i}
,

(2)

S\to B
is flat, proper, with fibers of pure dimension
1
.

The
b_{i}
and the intersection points of different irreducible components of
B
are the
pivot points
.

Definition 3.17
.

A
3
-nodal
(curve) singularity is a triple point that is locally isomorphic to
3
coordinate axes
(xy=yz=zx=0)\subset\mathbb{A}^{3}
.

Definition 3.18
.

A deformation equivalence as in Definition
3.16
is called
nodal
(or
2
-nodal
) if all fibers are nodal curves (singularities like
2
coordinate axes
(xy=0)\subset\mathbb{A}^{2}
).
We call a deformation
3
-nodal
if all but finitely many fibers are nodal curves, and the pivot fibers have only nodes and
3
-nodal points as singularities.

Remark 3.19
.

By
[
KT23
]
Example 19, every
3
-nodal deformation between nodal curves gives rise to a nodal deformation.

Lemma 3.20

(cf.
[
KT23
, (42.2)-(42.5)]
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety defined over an algebraically closed field
k
. Fix any positive integer
r
. There is a family of smooth curves in
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
:

\mathbf{L}_{r}\subset S\times X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
satisfying the following condition:

(1)

For any length
r
subscheme of
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
whose embedding dimension is
1
, the subfamily of curves that pass through this subscheme is non-empty and irreducible;

(2)

Let
M\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{r}\xrightarrow{F}X\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\sigma_{i}:M\to\mathcal{L},i=1,\ldots,r,
be the universal family parameterizing curves in
\mathbf{L}_{r}
with
r
-distinct points. Then the evaluation map

ev=\prod_{i=1}^{r}F\circ\sigma_{i}:M\to\prod_{i=1}^{r}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1})

is flat.

Proof.

Take a sufficiently ample linear system
|A|
on
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
and take the family of curves to be smooth complete intersections of divisors in
|A|
.
∎

We will call a curve in a chosen family
\mathbf{L}_{r}\subset S\times X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
an
\mathbf{L}_{r}
-curve
in the following.

Remark 3.21
.

The
H^{\text{ci}}
-family defined in
[
KT23
, (28)]
is a special case of
\mathbf{L}_{2}
-family. The difference is that we do not choose an explicit form of the ample divisor
A
.

Remark 3.22
.

In the following, we often need to lift finitely many families of curves
S\leftarrow Z_{i}\to X
to families of curves
S\to{Z}_{i}\to X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
such that the images of
{Z}_{i}
are disjoint.
One way to do this is to choose different points
p_{i}
in
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and use the compositions
Z_{i}\to X\cong X\times\{p_{i}\}\to X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
).

In the following, we will frequently use the construction of gluing families of nodal curves along smooth points. See
[
Knu83
, Theorem 3.4]
for detailed information.

Lemma 3.23

(cf.
[
KT23
, Corollary 29]
)
.

Keep the same notations as in Lemma
3.20
.
The family
\mathbf{L}_{r}\subset S\times X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
also satisfies the following property.

Let
U\leftarrow\mathcal{C}_{i}\xrightarrow{p_{i}}X,\sigma_{i}:U\to\mathcal{C}_{i},i=1,\ldots,r
be any
r
flat projective families of nodal curves with a marked point in the smooth locus.
Choose a lifting of
U\leftarrow\mathcal{C}_{i}\to X
to
U\leftarrow\mathcal{C}_{i}\xrightarrow{\tilde{p}_{i}}X\times\mathbb{P}^{1},
such that the images of
\tilde{p}_{i}\circ\sigma_{i}:U\to\mathcal{C}_{i}\to X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
are disjoint,
i=1,\ldots,r
(Remark
3.22
).
Assume that
U
is quasi-projective.
Choose finitely many points
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}
, and curves
L_{1},\ldots,L_{n}
in the family
\mathbf{L}_{r}
such that
L_{j}(1\leq j\leq n)
passes through the points
\tilde{p}_{1}(\sigma_{1}(u_{i})),\ldots,\tilde{p}_{1}(\sigma_{1}(u_{i}))(1\leq j\leq n)
.
After a generically finite dominant base change
f:V\to U
, we may construct a family of
\mathbf{L}_{r}
-curves with
r
-marked points,
V\leftarrow\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{p}X,\tau_{i}:V\to\mathcal{L},i=1,\ldots r,
such that

(1)

There are points
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in V
such that
f(v_{j})=u_{j}
and
\mathcal{L}_{v_{j}}=L_{j},1\leq j\leq n
.

(2)

p\circ\tau_{j}=p_{j}\circ\sigma_{j},1\leq j\leq n
.

As a result, one can glue the families
\mathcal{C}_{i},\mathcal{L}
over
V
along the sections
\sigma_{i}
and
\tau_{i}
to form a family of
connected
nodal curves.

Proof.

Fix a sufficiently ample divisor
A
and consider the universal family

CI

\displaystyle=\{(u,W)|u\in U,\tilde{p}_{i}\circ\sigma_{i}(u)\in\cap_{D\in W}D,W\subset{H^{0}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(A))},\dim W=d\}

\displaystyle\subset U\times\text{Grass}(d,H^{0}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(A))),d=\dim X.

Denote by
\textbf{CI}^{\text{sm}}
the locus where the base locus of the linear system
|W|
is a smooth complete intersection curve.
For
A
sufficiently ample, the fiber
\textbf{CI}^{\text{sm}}_{u}
is a dense open subset of the fiber
\textbf{CI}_{u}
for each
u\in U
.
Since we constructed
L_{i}
as complete intersection curves, each
L_{i}
corresponds to a unique
W_{i}\subset{H^{0}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(A))}
.
By taking general hyperplane sections of
\textbf{CI}^{\text{sm}}
containing the points
(u_{i},W_{i}),1\leq i\leq n
, we can construct a multisection
V\subset\textbf{CI}^{\text{sm}}\to U
. The morphism
f:V\to U
is the desired base change, and the family
\mathcal{L}\to V
is the family of complete intersection curves over
V
.
∎

Definition 3.24

(cf.
[
KT23
, Definition 30]
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety defined over an algebraically closed field
k
, and let
\mathbf{C}_{r}=\{\pi_{i}:C_{i}\to X,i=1,\ldots,r\}
be a set of
r
reduced curves of
X
.

Fix a family
\mathbf{L}_{r}\subset S\times X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
as Lemma
3.20
.
Denote by
S\leftarrow\mathbf{L}_{r}\xrightarrow{F}X
the family of curves obtained from projection to
X
.

Define

\text{Join}(\mathbf{C}_{r},\mathbf{L}_{r})

to be the scheme parameterizing curves of the form

\pi:(\coprod_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})\cup_{\sigma}(L,p_{1},\ldots,p_{r})\to X,

where
(L,p_{1},\ldots,p_{r})
is an
\mathbf{L}_{r}
-curve with
r
-distinct points

p_{1},\ldots,p_{r}
,

\sigma:\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{r}\}\to\coprod_{i=1}^{r}C_{i}

is an embedding such that
\sigma(p_{i})\in C_{i},\pi_{i}(\sigma(p_{i}))=F(p_{i})
, and the curve

\pi:(\coprod_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})\cup_{\sigma}(L,p_{1},\ldots,p_{r})\to X,

is glued from
(\coprod_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})\to X
and
L\to X
by identifying the closed subschemes
p_{i}\cong\sigma(p_{i})
.

We define

\text{Join}^{\text{nodal}}(\mathbf{C}_{r},\mathbf{L}_{r})

to be the subfamily of curves such that in addition,
\sigma(p_{i})\in C_{i}^{\text{sm}},i=1,\ldots,r
.

Remark 3.25
.

If each
C_{i}(1\leq i\leq r)
is a nodal curve, the curve
(\coprod_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})\cup_{\sigma}(L,p_{1},\ldots,p_{r})
is a curve with nodal or
3
-nodal singularities.
If in addition
\sigma(p_{i})\in C_{i}^{\text{sm}},i=1,\ldots,r
, the curve

(\coprod_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})\cup_{\sigma}(L,p_{1},\ldots,p_{r})
is a nodal curve
[
Knu83
, Theorem 3.4]
.

When
r=2
, the following is a special case of
[
KT23
, Corollary 31]
.

Lemma 3.26
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety defined over an algebraically closed field
k
.
Let
\mathbf{C}_{r}=\{\pi_{i}:C_{i}\to X,i=1,\ldots,r\}
be a set of
r
geometrically connected nodal curves in
X
.
Fix a family
\mathbf{L}_{r}\subset S\times X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
as in Definition
3.24
.

(1)

\text{Join}(\mathbf{C}_{r},\mathbf{L}_{r})
is connected.

(2)

\text{Join}^{\text{nodal}}(\mathbf{C}_{r},\mathbf{L}_{r})
is an open dense subset of
\text{Join}(\mathbf{C}_{r},\mathbf{L}_{r})
.

(3)

For any two members of the family of curves
\text{Join}^{\text{nodal}}(\mathbf{C}_{r},\mathbf{L}_{r})
, there is a
3
-nodal deformation from one to the other over a connected curve, which is obtained by gluing the constant deformation family of the curves
C_{1},\ldots C_{r}
to a family of deformations of curves in
\mathbf{L}_{r}
.
We can also modify the deformation at pivot points to make it nodal.

Proof of Lemma
3.26
.

Observe that that is a morphism

\Phi:\text{Join}(\textbf{C}_{r},\mathbf{L}_{r})\to\prod_{i=1}^{r}C_{i}

by mapping a curve
(\coprod_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})\cup_{\sigma}(L,p_{1},\ldots,p_{r})
to
\prod_{i}\sigma(p_{i})\in\prod C_{i}
.
The morphism
\Phi
is flat and has non-empty and geometrically irreducible fiber over any point by assumption on
\mathbf{L}_{r}
. This proves the first statement.
The second statement is true because
\text{Join}^{\text{nodal}}(\mathbf{C}_{r},\mathbf{L}_{r})=\Phi^{-1}(\prod_{i=1}^{r}C_{i}^{\text{sm}})
, which is dense in
\prod_{i=1}^{r}C_{i}
.

Finally, the last statement follows from the first two: take a connected curve in
\text{Join}(\mathbf{C}_{r},\mathbf{L}_{r})
and use Remarks
3.25
and
3.19
.
∎

3.3.
Proof of Theorems
3.10
and
3.11

In this section, we will often glue (families of) nodal curves together by identifying smooth points on them.
That is, given two families of nodal curves
S\leftarrow C\xrightarrow{f}X
and
S\leftarrow D\xrightarrow{g}X
, and finitely many sections
\sigma_{i}:S\to C,\tau_{i}:S\to D,1=1,\ldots,n
that lie in the smooth locus of
C\to S
and
D\to S
and such that
f\circ\sigma_{i}=g\circ\tau_{i}
, we glue
C,D
by identifying the subschemes
\sigma_{i}(S)\subset C
and
\tau_{i}(S)\subset D
for each
i
(
[
Knu83
, Theorem 3.4]
).
If it is not important to keep track of which points are identified,
we will simply use notations like
C\cup D
to denote the curve obtained by gluing
C
and
D
together along some smooth points.

In the proofs, we will usually make base changes several times.
To simplify the notations, we will use the same letter (usually
U
) to denote the base in the first few steps of base change.

The following observation is useful.

Construction 3.27

(Creating isomorphic base)
.

Given any two pointed quasi-projective algebraic sets of the same dimension
(B^{+},b_{0}^{+},b_{1}^{+}),(B^{-},b_{0}^{-},b_{1}^{-})
, we may take a general complete intersection
B
(which is of the same dimension as
B^{+}
and
B^{-}
) of
B^{+}\times B^{-}
that contains the two points
b_{0}=(b_{0}^{+},b_{0}^{-})
and
b_{1}=(b_{1}^{+},b_{1}^{-})
.
Then
(B,b_{0},b_{1})
maps to both
(B^{+},b_{0}^{+},b_{1}^{+}),(B^{-},b_{0}^{-},b_{1}^{-})
.

If both
B^{+}
and
B^{-}
are irreducible, up to replacing
B^{+/-}
with an alteration, we may always assume that the points
b_{i}^{+/-}
are smooth points.
Thus we may choose
B
to be smooth and irreducible.

If both
B^{+}
and
B^{-}
are connected curves, this construction is
[
KT23
, 26.2]
, and we may take
B
to be a connected curve.

The following is essentially
[
KT23
, Theorem 61]
.

Proposition 3.28

(cf.
[
KT23
, Theorem 61]
)
.

Keep the same notations as Theorem
3.10
. Assume furthermore that
U
is quasi-projective and that
\Gamma_{U}
, the family of cycles over
U
, is an effective nodal family of one-cycles.
Then there is a generically finite dominant base change
f:V\to U
, a connected two pointed quasi-projective curve
(T,t_{0},t_{1})
, two families of
\mathbf{L}_{s}
-curves
\mathcal{L}_{T}\to T
and
\mathcal{L}_{V}\to V
, a connected nodal curve
Z\to X
, two families of
r
-tuple of
L_{2}
-curves
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{V}\to V
,
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{T}\to T
, such that the followings hold.

(1)

There are two points
v_{0},v_{1}\in V
such that
f(v_{0})=u_{0}
,
f(v_{1})=u_{1}
.

(2)

We may glue the families of curves
\Gamma_{V}
,
\mathcal{L}_{V}
,
Z\times V
,
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{V}
together to a family of
connected
nodal curves
C_{V}
.

(3)

We may glue the families
\mathcal{L}_{T}
,
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{T}
,
Z\times T
, and a deformation over the image of
(\Gamma_{V}\cup\mathcal{L}_{V})|_{v_{0}}
to form a
3
-nodal deformation over
T
, which gives rise to a family of connected nodal curves
T\leftarrow C_{T}\to X
as in Remark
3.19
.

(4)

The cycle class of
C_{T}
at each point
t\in T
is
\gamma+[Z]+[\mathcal{L}_{T}|_{t}]+[r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{T}|_{t}]
.

(5)

The fiber of
C_{T}
over
t_{0}
(resp.
t_{1}
) is the same as the fiber of
C_{V}
over
v_{0}
(resp.
v_{1}
).

Proof.

We write
\Gamma_{U}=\sum m_{i}Z_{i},U\leftarrow Z_{i}\to X
.
Take
m_{i}
copies of each family of nodal curves
U\leftarrow Z_{i}\to X
.
Lift these
m_{i}
copies of
U\leftarrow Z_{i}\to X
for all
i
to families of nodal curves in
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
with disjoint images (Remark
3.22
).

We choose a family of
\mathbf{L}_{s}
-curves and glue them to these lifted families of nodal curves in
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
(up to a generically finite base change) as in Lemma
3.23
to produce a family of
connected
nodal curves.
We denote the family of
\mathbf{L}_{s}
-curves by
\mathcal{L}_{U}\to U
.
We call the resulting family of connected nodal curves
{\Gamma}_{U}\cup\mathcal{L}_{U}\to U
.
Moreover, we choose the family
\mathcal{L}_{U}\to U
so that the fibers
\mathcal{L}_{U}|_{u_{0}},\mathcal{L}_{U}|_{u_{1}}
are the same.

We use the equality of cycle classes
[({\Gamma}_{U}\cup\mathcal{L}_{U})|_{u_{0}}]=[({\Gamma}_{U}\cup\mathcal{L}_{U})|_{u_{1}}]
, and apply
[
KT23
, Corollary 35]
, which shows the following.

(1)

There is a copy of a fixed connected nodal curve
Z\to X
, connected to
({\Gamma}_{U}\cup\mathcal{L}_{U})|_{u_{0}}
by a curve
L_{0}
in an
H^{\text{ci}}
family (
[
KT23
, 28]
), which is an
\mathbf{L}_{2}
-curve (see Lemma
3.20
, Remark
3.21
).
Denote the resulting curve by
({\Gamma}_{U}\cup\mathcal{L}_{U})|_{u_{0}}\cup L_{0}\cup Z
.
And there is another copy of the fixed connected nodal curve
Z\to X
, connected to
({\Gamma}_{U}\cup\mathcal{L}_{U})|_{u_{1}}
by a curve
L_{1}
in the same
H^{\text{ci}}
(
=\mathbf{L}_{2}
) family as
L_{0}
. Denote by
({\Gamma}_{U}\cup\mathcal{L}_{U})|_{u_{1}}\cup L_{1}\cup Z
the resulting curve.

(2)

After further adding a suitable number of
H^{\text{ci}}
(
=\mathbf{L}_{2}
)-curves to both
({\Gamma}_{U}\cup\mathcal{L}_{U})|_{u_{0}}\cup L_{0}\cup Z
and
({\Gamma}_{U}\cup\mathcal{L}_{U})|_{u_{1}}\cup L_{1}\cup Z
, there is a
3
-nodal deformation between the resulting two curves over a two-pointed connected curve
({T},t_{0},t_{1})
, where
t_{0},t_{1}
are smooth points of
{T}
.
We use
C_{T}\to T
to denote the corresponding nodal family as in Remark
3.19
.

Finally, using Lemma
3.23
, after a generically finite base change, denoted by
f:V\to U
, we glue a family
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{V}\to V
of r-tuple of
\mathbf{L}_{2}
-curves over
V
,
Z\times V
,
(\Gamma_{U}\cup\mathcal{L}_{U})\times_{U}V
together to form a family of connected nodal curves
C_{V}\to V
so that
C_{V}|_{v_{0}}=C_{T}|_{t_{0}}
and
C_{V}|_{v_{1}}=C_{T}|_{t_{1}}
.
The statements (3) and (4) follow from
[
KT23
, Corollary 35.4, Complement 36]

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.10
.

First, observe that if
U^{\prime}\to U
is a birational morphism (not necessarily proper) and the statement holds for
U^{\prime}
(with any lifting of
u_{0},u_{1}
), then by the existence of a projective extension (Definition
3.8
and the remark after), the statement holds for
U
.

Based on this observation, using Nagata’s compactification and Chow lemma, we can assume that
U
is projective.

We write
\Gamma_{U}=\sum_{i}m_{i}Z_{i},Z_{i}\subset U\times X
.
Denote by
\tilde{Z}_{i}
the normalization of the component
Z_{i}
.
Up to a purely inseparable base change, we may assume that
\tilde{Z}_{i}\to Z_{i}\to U
is generically smooth for all
i
(apply
[
dJ97
, Lemma 2.8]
to the generic fiber of each component
Z_{i}
of the cycle
\Gamma_{U}
).

We make a generically finite projective base change
f^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\to U
and semistable reduction
[
dJ96
, Theorem 5.8]
to each component of
\Gamma_{U}
,
and thus produce a nodal family of one-cycles
U^{\prime}\leftarrow{\Gamma}_{U^{\prime}}\to X
such that
f^{\prime*}(\Gamma_{U})=[\Gamma_{U^{\prime}}]
as equi-dimensional family of one-cycles.
We may assume that
U^{\prime}
is smooth by the existence of an alteration.
The general fibers of each
\tilde{Z}_{i}\to U
are smooth and thus for any general point
u\in U
, and any point
u^{\prime}\in U^{\prime}
lying over
u
, the fibers
\tilde{Z}_{i}\times_{U}U^{\prime}|_{u^{\prime}}
and
\tilde{Z}_{i}|_{u}
are isomorphic.
Denote by
U^{0}
the open subset of
U
consisting of points
u\in U
satisfying the above condition.
Then for all points
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots
in
U^{\prime}
that is in the inverse image of
u\in U^{0}
, we have an equality of nodal one-cycles
\Gamma_{U^{\prime}}|_{x_{1}}=\Gamma_{U^{\prime}}|_{x_{2}}=\ldots
.

We write
\Gamma_{U^{\prime}}=\Gamma^{+}_{U^{\prime}}-\Gamma^{-}_{U^{\prime}}
, with
\Gamma^{+}_{U^{\prime}}
(resp.
\Gamma^{-}_{U^{\prime}}
) as its positive (resp. negative) part. We write

\gamma_{0}^{+}=\Gamma^{+}_{U^{\prime}}|_{u_{0}},\quad\gamma_{0}^{-}=\Gamma^{-}_{U^{\prime}}|_{u_{0}},\quad\gamma_{1}^{+}=\Gamma^{+}_{U^{\prime}}|_{u_{1}},\quad\gamma_{1}^{-}=\Gamma^{-}_{U^{\prime}}|_{u_{1}}.

By assumption,

[\gamma_{0}^{+}]-[\gamma_{0}^{-}]=[\gamma_{1}^{+}]-[\gamma_{1}^{-}]
.

We take a general complete intersection
V^{\prime}
(of the same dimension as
U^{\prime}
) containing
v_{0}^{\prime}=(u_{0},u_{1})
and
v_{1}^{\prime}=(u_{1},u_{0})
in the product
U^{\prime}\times U^{\prime}
.
Note that
V^{\prime}
is smooth, irreducible and projective (since
U^{\prime}
is smooth, irreducible and projective).
There are two nodal families of one-cycles

\Gamma_{p}=p^{*}\Gamma_{U^{\prime}}=\Gamma_{p}^{+}-\Gamma_{p}^{-},\quad\Gamma_{q}=q^{*}\Gamma_{U^{\prime}}=\Gamma_{q}^{+}-\Gamma_{q}^{-}

over
V^{\prime}
induced by pulling back
\Gamma_{U^{\prime}}
along the the two projections
p,q:V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}
, where
\Gamma_{p}^{+}
(resp.
\Gamma_{q}^{+}
) is the positive part of
\Gamma_{p}
(resp.
\Gamma_{q}
), and
\Gamma_{p}^{-}
(resp.
\Gamma_{q}^{-}
) is the negative part of
\Gamma_{p}
(resp.
\Gamma_{q}
).
We construct two new nodal families of one-cycles over
V^{\prime}
:

{\Gamma}_{V^{\prime}}^{+}=\Gamma_{p}^{+}+\Gamma_{q}^{-},\quad\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{-}=\Gamma_{p}^{-}+\Gamma_{q}^{-}.

We remark that
\Gamma_{p}=p^{*}\Gamma_{U^{\prime}}=\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{+}-\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{-}
.
The nodal one-cycle
\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{+}|_{v_{0}^{\prime}}
is
\gamma_{0}^{+}+\gamma_{1}^{-}
, and the nodal one-cycle
\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{+}|_{v_{1}^{\prime}}
is
\gamma_{1}^{+}+\gamma_{0}^{-}
.
Thus their cycle classes are the same.
It follows from a similar computation that the cycle class of the restriction of
\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{-}
to
v_{0}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime}
are the same.

So now we have two
effective
nodal families of one-cycles
\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{+},\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{-}
, and the restriction of each family to
v_{0}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime}
has the same cycle class.
We write
\tilde{p}:V^{\prime}\xrightarrow{p}U^{\prime}\to U
.

We apply Proposition
3.28
to
\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{+}
and
\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{-}
.
Using Construction
3.27
, we may take a single normal variety
V
and a connected two pointed projective curve
(T,t_{0},t_{1})
in Proposition
3.28
that works for both families
\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{+}
and
\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{-}
.
Denote by
f:(V,v_{0},v_{1})\to(U,u_{0},u_{1})
the base change.
For the family
\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{+}
(resp.
\Gamma_{V^{\prime}}^{-}
), denote by
Z^{+}
(resp.
Z^{-}
) the constant nodal curve,
\mathcal{L}_{V}^{+}
,
\mathcal{L}_{T}^{+}
(resp.
\mathcal{L}_{V}^{-}
,
\mathcal{L}_{T}^{-}
) the family of
\mathbf{L}_{s}
-curves over
V
and
T
,
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{V}^{+}
,
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{T}^{+}
(resp.
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{V}^{-}
,
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{T}^{-}
) the family of
\mathbf{L}_{2}
-curves over
V
and
T
,
C_{V}^{+}
(resp.
C_{V}^{-}
) the family of nodal curves over
V
glued from above families,
C_{T}^{+}
(resp.
C_{T}^{-}
) the family of nodal curves over
T
glued from above families and deformation over the image of
(\Gamma_{V}^{+}\cup\mathcal{L}_{V}^{+})|_{v_{0}}
(resp.
(\Gamma_{V}^{-}\cup\mathcal{L}_{V}^{-})|_{v_{0}}
).

We define

\Gamma_{V}=(C_{V}^{+}-\mathcal{L}_{V}^{+}-r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{V}^{+}-Z^{+}\times V)-(C_{V}^{-}-\mathcal{L}_{V}^{-}-r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{V}^{-}-Z^{-}\times V),

\Gamma_{T}=(C_{T}^{+}-\mathcal{L}_{T}^{+}-r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{T}^{+}-Z^{+}\times T)-(C_{T}^{-}-\mathcal{L}_{T}^{-}-r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{T}^{-}-Z^{-}\times T).

By Proposition
3.28
.(4), the family of cycles
[C_{T}^{+}]
is a fixed cycle plus deformations in the families
\mathbf{L}_{s}
and
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|
.
In the definition of
\Gamma_{T}
, we subtract the deformations of cycles in the families
\mathbf{L}_{s}
and
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|
in
[C_{T}^{+}]
.
The same is true for the family
[C_{T}^{-}]\to T
.
Therefore,
[\Gamma_{T}]\to T
is a family of constant cycles.

By abuse of notations, we still denote the pull-back families of
\Gamma_{p},\Gamma_{q}
along the morphism
V\to V^{\prime}
by
\Gamma_{p}
and
\Gamma_{q}
.

Choose a point
u\in U^{0}
and let
v,v^{\prime}
be two points in
f^{-1}(u)
.
Our next goal is to construct the family of constant cycles over a curve connecting
v,v^{\prime}
.

Choose an irreducible smooth two pointed curve
(B_{v,v^{\prime}},b,b^{\prime})
mapping to
(V,v,v^{\prime})
and denote the pull-back of the family
\Gamma_{q}^{-}\to V
by
\Gamma_{B_{v,v^{\prime}}}\to B_{v,v^{\prime}}
.

The fiber of
C_{V}^{+}
over
v
(resp.
v^{\prime}
) is a nodal curve of the form

{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v}\cup\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v}\cup L_{v}\cup Z^{+}\cup r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v}

(resp.

{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v^{\prime}}\cup\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v^{\prime}}\cup L_{v^{\prime}}\cup Z^{+}\cup r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v^{\prime}}
).
Here
{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v}
and
{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v^{\prime}}
are by definition effective nodal one-cycles.
But we abuse the notations by treating them as disconnected nodal curves consisting of the disjoint union of
m_{i}
copies of the nodal curve
\tilde{Z}_{i}|_{u}
.
We think of the fiber of
C_{V}^{+}
over
v
(resp.
v^{\prime}
) as nodal curves
Z^{+}
and
{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v},\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v}
(resp.
{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v^{\prime}},\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v^{\prime}}
), connected by two types of curves:

•

an
\mathbf{L}_{s}
-curve
L_{v}
(resp.
L_{v^{\prime}}
) connecting
{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v}
and
\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v}
(resp.
{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v^{\prime}}
and
\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v^{\prime}}
),

•

r
-tuple of
\mathbf{L}_{2}
-curves
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v}
(resp.
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v^{\prime}}
) connecting
{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v}\cup\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v}\cup L_{v}
and
Z^{+}

(resp.

{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v^{\prime}}\cup\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v^{\prime}}\cup L_{v^{\prime}}
and
Z^{+}
).

We first apply Lemma
3.23
(once for the family
\mathbf{L}_{s}
and
r
-times for the family
\mathbf{L}_{2}
) to the families
\Gamma_{B_{v,v^{\prime}}}\to B_{v,v^{\prime}}
,
\Gamma_{p}^{+}|_{v}\times B_{v,v^{\prime}}\to B_{v,v^{\prime}}
,
Z^{+}\times B_{v,v^{\prime}}\to B_{v,v^{\prime}}
to construct a deformation
C^{q+}_{v,v^{\prime}}\to(B^{+}_{v,v^{\prime}},b_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v},b_{v,v^{\prime}}^{q})
from the curve

C^{q+}_{v,v^{\prime}}|_{b_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v}}={\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v}\cup\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v}\cup L_{v}\cup Z^{+}\cup r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v}

to a curve of the form

C_{q}^{+}=C^{q+}_{v,v^{\prime}}|_{b_{v,v^{\prime}}^{q}}={\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v}\cup\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v^{\prime}}\cup L\cup Z^{+}\cup L^{\prime}.

where
L
is an
\mathbf{L}_{s}
-curve and
L^{\prime}
consists of r-tuple of
|\mathbf{L}_{2}|
-curves.

Recall that by the choice of
u
,
\Gamma_{p}^{+}|_{v}=\Gamma_{p}^{+}|_{v^{\prime}}
.
We deform the curve
L
and each irreducible component of
L^{\prime}
over a connected curve using Lemma
3.26
(first apply for the family
\mathbf{L}_{s}
and then apply r times for the family
\mathbf{L}_{2}
) to construct a nodal deformation
C^{L+}_{v,v^{\prime}}\to(T^{+}_{v,v^{\prime}},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{q},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})
from

C_{q}^{+}={\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v}\cup\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v^{\prime}}\cup L\cup Z^{+}\cup L^{\prime}

to

{\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v^{\prime}}\cup\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v^{\prime}}\cup L_{v^{\prime}}\cup Z^{+}\cup r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v^{\prime}}.

Here we first produce a
3
-nodal deformation
constructed by gluing the constant families
({\Gamma}_{p}^{+}|_{v}\cup\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{v^{\prime}})\times T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+},Z\times T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}
, and two deformations
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v,v^{\prime}}\to T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+},\mathcal{L}_{v,v^{\prime}}\to T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{\prime+}
of the curves in the families
r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|
and
\mathbf{L}_{s}
.
Then we use Remark
3.19
to turn it into a nodal deformation.

We then do the same type of deformation for the curves
C_{V}^{-}|_{v},C_{V}^{-}|_{v^{\prime}}
using the family
\Gamma_{B_{v,v^{\prime}}}\times_{B_{v,v^{\prime}}}B^{+}_{v,v^{\prime}}\to B^{+}_{v,v^{\prime}}
and deformations of curves in the family
\mathbf{L}_{s}
and
\mathbf{L}_{2}
.
Again the deformation consists of two steps: first a deformation over an irreducible curve
C^{q-}_{v,v^{\prime}}\to B^{-}_{v,v^{\prime}}
and then a deformation over a connected curve
C^{L-}_{v,v^{\prime}}\to T^{-}_{v,v^{\prime}}
.
Note that there is also a deformation
C^{q+}_{v,v^{\prime}}\times_{B^{+}_{v,v^{\prime}}}B^{-}_{v,v^{\prime}}\to B^{-}_{v,v^{\prime}}
.

By Construction
3.27
, we may assume that
T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}
maps to
T^{+}_{v,v^{\prime}}
and thus we have the pull-back deformation
C_{v,v^{\prime}}^{L+}\times_{T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}}T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}\to T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}
.

We glue
T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}
and
B_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}
together by identifying the two points
b_{v,v^{\prime}}^{q}
and
t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{q}
, the fiber over which is the nodal curve
C_{q}^{+}
.
We call the resulting curve
T_{v,v^{\prime}}

and we take
t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v}
to be
b_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v}
.
We glue the two families
C^{q+}_{v,v^{\prime}}\times_{B^{+}_{v,v^{\prime}}}B^{-}_{v,v^{\prime}}
and
C_{v,v^{\prime}}^{L+}\times_{T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}}T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}
together (along the nodal curve
C^{+}_{q}
) to a nodal deformation family over the connected two pointed curve
(T_{v,v^{\prime}},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})\leftarrow C^{+}_{v,v^{\prime}}\to X
.
In a similar way, by gluing the two families
C^{q-}_{v,v^{\prime}}
and
C_{v,v^{\prime}}^{L-}
together (along the nodal curve
C^{-}_{q}
), we construct a nodal deformation family over the connected two pointed curve
(T_{v,v^{\prime}},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})\leftarrow C_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}\to X
.

We take the nodal family of one-cycles over
T_{v,v^{\prime}}
to be

\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}=(C_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}-r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}-Z^{+}\times T_{v,v^{\prime}}-\mathcal{L}_{v,v^{\prime}})-(C_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}-r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}-Z^{-}\times T_{v,v^{\prime}}-\mathcal{L}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}).

Over the subcurve
B^{-}_{v,v^{\prime}}\subset T_{v,v^{\prime}}
, the family of cycles
\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}
is

[\Gamma_{p}^{+}|_{v}+\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{b}+Z^{+}+\mathcal{L}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}|_{b}+r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}|_{b}].

The family of cycles
\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}
is

[\Gamma_{p}^{-}|_{v}+\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{b}+Z^{-}+\mathcal{L}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}|_{b}+r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}|_{b}].

The varying family of cycles
[\Gamma_{q}^{-}|_{b}]
appears in both the positive and negative side.
Thus they do not contribute to the family of cycles
[\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}]
.
In the construction of
\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}
, we subtract the varying families of cycles
[\mathcal{L}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}|_{b}+r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}|_{b}]
(resp.
[\mathcal{L}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}|_{b}+r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}|_{b}]
) from
[C_{v,v^{\prime}}^{+}]
(resp.
[C^{-}_{v,v^{\prime}}]
).
Therefore the family of cycles
[\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}]
is constant.
By a similar computation, for the subcurve
T_{v,v^{\prime}}^{-}\subset T_{v,v^{\prime}}
,
the family of cycles
[\Gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}]
is constant.

Now we apply projective extension (
3.8
) to the nodal families over
V,T,T_{v,v^{\prime}}
. By abuse of notations, we use the same letters to denote the extensions.
Then we achieve that
f:V\to U
is projective, and that
T,T_{v,v^{\prime}}
are connected projective curves.

To construct
W
, we note that the nodal families over the pair of points
(v_{0},t_{0})
(resp.
(v_{1},t_{1})
,
(v,t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v})
,
(v^{\prime},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})
) are the same.
So we glue the schemes
V,T,T_{v,v^{\prime}}
together by identifying the pair of points
(v_{0},t_{0})
(resp.
(v_{1},t_{1})
,
(v,t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v})
,
(v^{\prime},t_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})
).
We take
W
to be the resulting scheme and it carries a nodal family obtained by gluing the nodal families over
V,T,T_{v,v^{\prime}}
together.
∎

Remark 3.29
.

We observe the following interesting phenomenon.
Over the curves
T,T_{v,v^{\prime}}
, the families of cycles are constant, but both the positive and negative part are varying as families of cycles.
Over the curve
T
, this is due to the fact that we deform curves in
\mathbf{L}_{s},r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|
to construct the family of curves
C_{T},C_{v,v^{\prime}}
, and thus the positive part of the family of cycles is a constant one-cycle plus deformation of cycles in the family
\mathbf{L}_{s},r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|
. Similarly, the negative part is also a constant one-cycle plus deformation of cycles in the families
\mathbf{L}_{s},r|\mathbf{L}_{2}|
.
Over the curve
T_{v,v^{\prime}}
, there is also a deformation in the family
\Gamma_{q}^{-}
on both the positive and negative side.

Proof of Theorem
3.11
.

We first apply Theorem
3.10
.
We use
V^{s}
(resp.
T^{s}
,
T^{s}_{v,v^{\prime}}
,
U^{s}
,
W^{s}
) to denote
V
(resp.
T
,
T_{v,v^{\prime}}
,
U^{0}
,
W
) in the statement of Theorem
3.10
.
Choose an embedding of each component of
\Gamma_{W^{s}}
into
\mathbb{P}^{M}
.
This gives a morphism

\Phi:V^{s}\coprod T^{s}\coprod(\coprod_{f^{s}(v)=f^{s}(v^{\prime})=u}T^{s}_{v,v^{\prime}})\to W^{s}\to\prod_{i}\text{Hilb}^{\text{nodal}}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{M}),

where
\text{Hilb}^{\text{nodal}}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{M})
is the open locus of the Hilbert scheme of
X\times\mathbb{P}^{M}
parameterizing curves with at worst nodal singularities.
Denote by
\pi_{i}
the projection from
\text{Hilb}^{\text{nodal}}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{M})
to the
i
-th factor and
\Phi_{i}=\pi_{i}\circ\Phi
.
Denote by
W_{i}
the image of
\Phi_{i}
.
Each
W_{i}
is connected.

Denote by
\mathbf{T}\subset\text{Hilb}^{\text{nodal}}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{M})
the family of curves constructed in
[
KT23
, Theorem 44]
.
Since
\mathbf{T}
parameterizes free curves (
[
KT23
, (41.2)]
), it is contained in the smooth locus of a unique irreducible component of the Hilbert scheme, denoted by
H_{\mathbf{T}}
(
[
KT23
, Claim 41.4]
).

Let
\text{EComb}^{d-\text{free}}(W_{i},r\mathbf{T})
be space parameterizing family of embedded combs with handles parameterized by the image
W_{i}
and
r
-teeth in
T
, and that are
d
-free along the handles and free
[
KT23
, Definition 45, (41.2)]
.
Each
\text{EComb}^{d-\text{free}}(W_{i},r\mathbf{T})
admits a morphism to the
r
-fold product
H_{\mathbf{T}}^{r}
.
There is also a morphism
\Pi_{i}:\text{EComb}^{d-\text{free}}(W_{i},r\mathbf{T})\to W_{i}
, which, by
[
KT23
, Theorem 48]
, is surjective, has connected fibers, and satisfies the curve lifting property (
[
KT23
, Definition 47]
).
As a result, we may find a quasi-projective variety
\tilde{V}
(resp. two pointed connected quasi-projective curve
(\tilde{T},\tilde{t}_{0},\tilde{t}_{1})
,
(\tilde{T}_{v,v^{\prime}},\tilde{t}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v},\tilde{t}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})
) with a generically finite dominant morphism
\tilde{V}\to V^{s}
(resp. a morphism of two pointed curves
(\tilde{T},\tilde{t}_{0},\tilde{t}_{1})\to({T}^{s},{t}^{s}_{0},{t}^{s}_{1})
,
(\tilde{T}_{v,v^{\prime}},\tilde{t}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v},\tilde{t}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{v^{\prime}})\to({T}^{s}_{v,v^{\prime}},{t}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{s,v},{t}_{v,v^{\prime}}^{s,v^{\prime}})),
which induces a nodal family of one-cycles
\tilde{V}\leftarrow\Gamma_{\tilde{V}}\to X
(resp.
\tilde{T}\leftarrow\Gamma_{\tilde{T}_{v,v^{\prime}}}\to X
,
\tilde{T}_{v,v^{\prime}}\leftarrow\Gamma_{\tilde{T}}\to X
), and a morphism

\tilde{\Phi}:\tilde{V}\coprod\tilde{T}\coprod(\coprod_{v,v^{\prime}}\tilde{T}_{v,v^{\prime}})\to\prod_{i}\text{EComb}^{d-\text{free}}(W_{i},r\mathbf{T})

such that the following diagram commutes

\begin{CD}\tilde{V}\coprod\tilde{T}\coprod(\coprod_{v,v^{\prime}}\tilde{T}_{v,v^{\prime}})@>{\tilde{\Phi}}>{}>\prod_{i}\text{EComb}^{d-\text{free}}_{i}(W_{i},r\mathbf{T})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
V^{s}\coprod T^{s}\coprod(\coprod_{v,v^{\prime}}T^{s}_{v,v^{\prime}})@>{(\prod\Phi_{i})}>{}>\prod_{i}W_{i}\end{CD}

Since
W_{i}
is connected, there is a unique geometrically irreducible component
H_{i}
of
\text{Hilb}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{M})
containing
\text{EComb}^{d-\text{free}}(W_{i},r\mathbf{T})
(
[
KT23
, Corollary 49]
).

In positive characteristic, we take
W
to be the normalization of
\prod_{i}(H_{i}\times\prod_{j=1}^{r}H_{\mathbf{T}})
.
In characteristic
0
, we take a resolution of singularities
W\to\prod_{i}(H_{i}\times\prod_{j=1}^{r}H_{\mathbf{T}})
that is an isomorphism over the smooth locus.
The normalization/resolution of singularities is an isomorphism in a neighbourhood of the image of
\text{EComb}^{d-\text{free}}_{i}(W_{i},r\mathbf{T})
.
The universal family of subschemes induces an equi-dimensional family of one-cycles over
H_{i},H_{\mathbf{T}}^{r}
.
Denote by
H_{i}\leftarrow\Gamma_{i}\to X
and
H_{\mathbf{T}}\leftarrow\Gamma_{\mathbf{T}}\to X
the corresponding equi-dimensional family of one-cycles.
Then
W
carries an equi-dimensional family of one-cycles

\Gamma_{W}=\sum m_{i}p_{i}^{*}\Gamma_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{r}p_{ij}^{*}\Gamma_{\mathbf{T}}
, where
p_{i}:W\to H_{i}
and
p_{ij}:W\to H_{\mathbf{T}}
are the natural morphisms to each factor of
\prod_{i}(H_{i}\times\prod_{j=1}^{r}H_{\mathbf{T}})
.
The cycle class of the nodal family of one-cycles on
\tilde{V}\coprod\tilde{T}\coprod(\coprod_{v,v^{\prime}}\tilde{T}_{v,v^{\prime}})
equals the pull-back of
\Gamma_{W}
.

Denote by
\tilde{f}
the morphism
\tilde{V}\to U
.
We take
{V}
to be a normal partial compactification of
\tilde{V}
such that the morphism
\tilde{f}:\tilde{V}\to V^{s}
extends to a projective morphism
f_{V/V^{s}}:{V}\to V^{s}
, and such that the morphism
\tilde{V}\to W
extends to
V\to W
(e.g. take
V
to be the normalization of the graph closure of
\tilde{V}\subset V^{s}\times W\times\mathbb{P}^{N}
after choosing a locally closed embedding
\tilde{V}\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
), as shown in the following diagram.

\textstyle{{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{projective morphism }f_{V/V^{s}}}
-

\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}}
\textstyle{\prod_{i}\text{EComb}^{d-\text{free}}(W_{i},r\mathbf{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
