162.9504pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 162.9504pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H_{2}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})[\ell^{n}]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The left vertical map is the composition of cycle class map
cl^{S}_{1,1}
for
S
and action of the correspondence
\Gamma_{S}
on cohomology.
The middle and the right vertical maps are (higher) cycle class maps.

The left square is commutative by Proposition
2.19
. The right square is commutative by Proposition
2.21
and the fact that cycle class map is trivial on cycles that are algebraically equivalent to
0
.

The first observation is that the image of left and middle vertical maps actually lands in a smaller group.

Claim 5.18
.

The image of the higher cycle class map

cl_{1,1}^{X}:\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

is
\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\subset H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
.

Proof.

We first note that

cl_{1,1}^{X}:\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

factors through
\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\subset H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
.
This is because any element in
\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
is represented by a family of one-cycles over
\Delta^{1}\cong\mathbb{A}^{1}
, and thus comes from
\text{CH}_{1}(\Sigma,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
for some surface
\Sigma
(which can be taken as the closure in
X
of the locus swept out by the support of this family of one-cycles over
\Delta^{1}
). Therefore the higher cycle class map factors through
\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
by the functoriality of higher cycle class maps under proper push-forward (Proposition
2.15
).

Since the higher cycle class map is compatible with proper push-forward (Proposition
2.15
), we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\oplus_{\Sigma}\text{CH}_{1}(\Sigma,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})@>{cl_{1,1}^{\Sigma}}>{\cong}>\oplus_{\Sigma}H_{3}(\Sigma,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})@>{cl_{1,1}^{X}}>{}>\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}),\end{CD}

where the direct sum is over all
2
-dimensional finite type connected reduced schemes
\Sigma
with a proper morphism to
X
.
In the commutative diagram,
the upper horizontal arrow is an isomorphism induced by the higher cycle class map (Lemma
5.17
),
and the right vertical arrow is surjective by the definition of
\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
. Therefore the map

\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

is surjective.
∎

Claim 5.19
.

The map

\Gamma_{S*}\circ cl^{S}_{1,1}:CH_{0}(S,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

factors through
H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\subset H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
.

Proof.

For any variety
Y
,
we have a short exact sequence

0\to H_{1}(Y,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to H_{1}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H_{0}(Y,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})[\ell^{n}]\to 0.

Therefore
H_{1}(Y,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\xrightarrow{\cong}H_{1}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
,
since
H_{0}(Y,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
is torsion free.

The cycle class map induces an isomorphism (Corollary
2.17
)

cl_{0,1}:CH_{0}(S,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\xrightarrow{\cong}H_{1}(S,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\xrightarrow{\cong}H_{1}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}.

This fact, combined with the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}H_{1}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})@>{\mod\ell^{n}}>{}>H_{1}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}@>{\cong}>{}>H_{1}(S,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\\
@V{}V{\Gamma_{S*}}V@V{}V{\Gamma_{S*}}V@V{}V{\Gamma_{S*}}V\\
H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})@>{\mod\ell^{n}}>{}>H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}@>{}>{}>H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}),\end{CD}

shows that the left vertical arrow in the commutative diagram (
9
) is the composition

CH_{0}(S,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\xrightarrow{\cong}H_{1}(S,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\cong H_{1}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\xrightarrow{\Gamma_{S*}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}).

This proves the claim.
∎

Furthermore, the image of

\Gamma_{S*}\circ cl^{S}_{1,1}:CH_{0}(S,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

is also contained in
\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
since the middle vertical map has image contained in it (Claim
5.18
).
So we have an induced commutative diagram:

(10)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 75.70718pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-54.10156pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\oplus_{(S,\Gamma_{S})}CH_{0}(S,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.06506pt\raise 5.89445pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.8889pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\oplus\Gamma_{S*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.70718pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.23114pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\oplus\Gamma_{S*}\circ cl_{1,1}^{S}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 99.70718pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 200.37274pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 138.03996pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.23114pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{cl_{1,1}^{X}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 138.03996pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 200.37274pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A_{1}(X)[\ell^{n}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\to 0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 233.14995pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{cl_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 233.14995pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-75.70718pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.70718pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 104.2905pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 104.2905pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 201.2477pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 201.2477pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H_{2}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})[\ell^{n}]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where the direct sum is taken over isomorphism classes of equi-dimensional families of one-cycles over smooth projective varieties.

By Theorem
5.12
, the upper row is exact. The lower row is also exact, since it comes from

0\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H_{2}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})[\ell^{n}]\to 0.

We can be even more precise about the image of the left vertical map.

Claim 5.20
.

The image of the left vertical map in the commutative diagram (
10
) equals the image of
\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
.

Proof of Claim
5.20
.

By the proof of Claim
5.19
, the left vertical arrow in the commutative diagram (
10
) is the direct sum of the compositions

CH_{0}(S,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\xrightarrow{\cong}H_{1}(S,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\cong H_{1}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\xrightarrow{\Gamma_{S*}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}.

Therefore, by Lemma
2.29
, the left vertical map in the commutative diagram (
10
) has the same image as

\tilde{N}_{1,\text{cyl}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}).

By Lemma
2.33
,
\tilde{N}_{1,\text{cyl}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})=\tilde{N}^{d-2}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
. This proves the claim.
∎

Note that
\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\subset\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\subset H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
are finitely generated
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-modules. Thus we have isomorphisms

\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\xrightarrow{\cong}\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n},\quad\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\xrightarrow{\cong}\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}.

We have a factorization

\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\subset H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}.

Taking inverse limit, we get maps

\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}(H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}))\subset H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}).

Here the last inclusion is preserved due to left exactness of inverse limit.

Since the compositions

\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})

are the inclusions of subgroups, we have the following claim.

Claim 5.21
.

We have inclusions

\tilde{N}\text{H}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\subset\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\subset\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\subset H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})

Now our next claim is:

Claim 5.22
.

The cokernel of the inclusion

(11)

\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

is torsion free.
So is the cokernel of the inclusion

\tilde{N}^{d-2}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to N^{d-2}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}).

Proof of Claim
5.22
.

By the snake lemma and Claims
5.18
,
5.20
, we have an exact sequence:

(12)

\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to C_{n}\to 0,

where

C_{n}
is the cokernel of

(13)

\alpha_{n}:\text{Ker}(\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}))\to\text{Ker}(A_{1}[\ell^{n}]\to H_{2}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})[\ell^{n}]).

Since
C_{n}
is a quotient of
\text{Ker}(A_{1}[\ell^{n}]\to H_{2}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})[\ell^{n}])
, the kernel of connecting maps
C_{n+m}\to C_{n}
contains the
\ell^{m}
-torsions
C_{n+m}[\ell^{m}]
.
Therefore the inverse limit
\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}C_{n}
is torsion free.

Every term involved in the exact sequence (
12
) is a finite abelian group, and thus the exact sequence (
12
) satisfies the Mittag-Leffler condition and the inverse limit of (
12
) is exact.
So the cokernel of

\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

is
\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}C_{n}
and torsion free.

In view of the inclusions in Claim
5.21
, the cokernel of
\tilde{N}H_{3}\to\text{NH}_{3}
is a sub-
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-module of the cokernel of
\tilde{N}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
, and thus also torsion free. This proves the claim.
∎

On the other hand, we know that the quotient
N^{d-2}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\tilde{N}^{d-2}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
is torsion since the strong coniveau filtration and coniveau filtration agree for
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
-coefficient.
So it has to be zero.
That is, the two subgroups coincide:

\tilde{N}^{d-2}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})=N^{d-2}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}).

∎

For later use, we note the following.

Claim 5.23
.

Assume that
X
is SRC in codimension one. The inclusions

(14)

\tilde{N}^{d-2}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to N^{d-2}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\xrightarrow{\psi}\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

The first isomorphism is already shown above.
We have already shown that the second map is injective (Claim
5.21
).
The cokernel of
\psi
is torsion since it is a subgroup of the cokernel of
N^{d-2}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
, which is torsion by Corollary
2.35
. By the first isomorphism and the fact that the composition has torsion free cokernel (Claim
5.22
), the cokernel of
\psi
is also torsion free, and thus zero.
∎

Remark 5.24
.

If
X
is a threefold that is SRC in codimension one, the proof of Theorem
5.16
can be simplified. In fact, in this case, we know that
A_{1}(X)[\ell^{n}]\to H_{2}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})[\ell^{n}]
is injective by Bloch-Srinivas
[
BS83
, Theorem 1, (ii)]
. So

\tilde{N}^{1}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap\text{NH}_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

is surjective by the snake lemma and Claim
5.18
. The surjectivity is preserved when taking inverse limit since the groups are finite and satisfy the Mittag-Leffler condition. Then

\tilde{N}^{1}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to N^{1}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\cap N^{1}H_{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

are isomorphisms (by claim
5.21
).

Lemma 5.25
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective
3
-fold defined over an algebraically closed field that is separably rationally connected in codimension one. Fix a prime number
\ell
different from the characteristic of
k
. Then

N^{1}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})=H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})

and the higher cycle class map induces a surjective map

\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}\text{CH}_{1}({X},1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})\to H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2)).

Proof.

By Corollary
2.9
, the quotient
H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/N^{1}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
is torsion free.
On the other hand, by Corollary
2.35
, the quotient
H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/N^{1}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
is torsion. So it must be zero.

Claim
5.18
shows that the higher cycle class map

cl_{1,1}^{X}:\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to N^{1}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})

is surjective.
By Corollary
2.12
, we have

\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\cong H^{3}(X,\tau^{\leq 2}R\pi_{*}{\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes 2}}),

where
\pi:X_{\text{\'{e}t}}\to X_{\text{Zar}}
is the morphism from the étale site to the Zariski site and
\tau
is the good truncation functor.
It follows that the higher cycle class map

\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{3}(X,\tau^{\leq 2}R\pi_{*}{\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes 2}})\to H^{3}(X,R\pi_{*}{\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes 2}})\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes 2})

is an injection,
since
H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,R^{3}\pi_{*}{\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes 2}}[-3])=0
.
Therefore the higher cycle class map induces an isomorphism

cl_{1,1}^{X}:\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\xrightarrow{\cong}N^{1}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}).

We have maps between finite abelian groups

N^{1}H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})/\ell^{n}\to N^{1}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}).

Taking inverse limits, we get

N^{1}H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}N^{1}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\cong\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}).

Since
N^{1}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})=H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
, we have the desired surjection

\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}\text{CH}_{1}(X,1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2)).

∎

Combined with Theorem
5.16
, this implies the following.

Theorem 5.26
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective
3
-fold over an algebraically closed field.
Fix a prime number
\ell
different from the characteristic of
k
.
Assume that
X
is separably rationally connected in codimension one. Then the following subgroups on
H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
introduced in Definition
2.23
equal the whole cohomology group:

\tilde{N}_{1,\text{cyl}}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})=\tilde{N}^{1}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})=N^{1}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})=H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}).

5.5.
Arithmetic part of the cycle class map

Over finite fields, we have the following corollary of Theorem
5.16
.

Corollary 5.27
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety of dimension
d
defined over a finite field
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, that is separably rationally connected in codimension one.
Fix a prime number
\ell
different from the characteristic of
k
.
Assume one of the followings

(1)

N^{d-2}H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))=H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))
.

(2)

The higher cycle class map induces a surjective map

cl:\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}\text{CH}_{1}(\overline{X},1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1)).

Then every class in
H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1)))
is the class of an algebraic cycle defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.
In particular, this holds if
X
has dimension
3
.

Proof of Corollary
5.27
.

We first show that the surjectivity of the map

cl:\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}\text{CH}_{1}(\overline{X},1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))

implies that
N^{d-2}H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))=H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))
.
In fact, we have a factorization of the above map

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}\text{CH}_{1}(\overline{X},1,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}N^{d-2}H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d-1))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d-1))=H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1)).

Therefore the map

\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}N^{d-2}H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d-1))\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d-1))

is surjective. On the other hand, since
N^{d-2}H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
is a subgroup of
H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})
, the inverse limit is injective, hence an isomorphism.
We have an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))/\ell^{n}\cap N^{d-2}H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d-1))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi}

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}N^{d-2}H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d-1))\to\lim\limits_{\xleftarrow[n]{}}H^{2d-2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))[\ell^{n}],

where the first inverse limit is
N^{d-2}H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))
by Claim
5.23
, and the last inverse limit is torsion free.
Since the quotient

H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))/N^{d-2}H_{\text{\'{e}t}}^{2d-3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))

is torsion by Corollary
2.35
, we know that
\phi
is an isomorphism and thus

N^{d-2}H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})

is an isomorphism.

By Theorem
5.16
, the two subgroups
N^{d-2}H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}),\tilde{N}^{d-2}H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
agree. So they equal the whole cohomology group
H^{2d-3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
by the previous paragraph.
Then the corollary follows from
[
SS23
, Proposition 5.6]
, the statement of which is recalled in Proposition
1.18
.

The
3
-fold case follows from Theorem
5.26
.
∎

6.
The integral Tate conjecture, the local-global principle, and the unramified cohomology

For general discussions on the relation between the integral Tate conjecture, unrammified cohomology, and the local-global principle for zero-cycles over global function fields, one can consult
[
CTK13
,
CTS23
]
.

6.1.
The integral Tate conjecture and the local-global principle for zero-cycles

Let
X
be a smooth projective geometrically irreducible variety of dimension
d
defined over a finite field
\mathbb{F}
. We have the cycle class maps:

(15)

\text{CH}^{r}(X)\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{2r}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(r)),

\text{CH}^{r}(X)\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{2r}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(r))\to H^{2r}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(r))^{G}.

We also have the corresponding cycle class maps after tensoring with
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
.
The Tate conjecture predicts that the cycle class map on codimension
r
cycles

\text{CH}^{r}(X)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}\to H^{2r}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(r))

is surjective for any smooth projective variety
X
defined over a finite field.
While the cycle class map (
15
) is in general not surjective for
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
coefficients (
[
CTS10
, Théorème 2.1]
), one is still interested in knowing in which cases surjectivity still hold.
This is usually called the integral Tate conjecture (even though it is not true in general).

Of particular interest to this paper is the following.

Conjecture 6.1
.

For a smooth projective variety
X
defined over
\mathbb{F}
, the cycle class map for one-cycles (i.e. (
15
) for
r=d-1
) is surjective.

See Section
8
for the explanation why we expect this integral Tate conjecture for one-cycles to be true for varieties that are SRC in codimension one.

The connection between the integral Tate conjecture for one-cycles and Conjectures
1.1
,
1.2
is the following.

Theorem 6.2

(
[
CT99
, Proposition 3.2]
,
[
Sai89
, Corollary (8-6)]
)
.

Let
\mathbb{F}
be a finite field,
C
a smooth projective geometrically connected curve over
\mathbb{F}
, and
K
the function field of
C
.
Let
\mathcal{X}
be a smooth projective geometrically connected variety of dimension
d+1
defined over
\mathbb{F}
, equipped with a morphism
p:\mathcal{X}\to C
, whose generic fiber
X
is smooth and geometrically irreducible. Let
l
be a prime different from the characteristic.

(1)

If the cycle class map

\text{CH}^{d}(\mathcal{X})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{2d}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))

is surjective, Conjectures
1.1
and
1.2
are true for the generic fiber
X
.

(2)

If the cycle class map

\text{CH}^{d}(\mathcal{X})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{2d}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\to H^{2d}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))^{G}

is surjective, or if

\text{CH}^{d}(\mathcal{X})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{2d}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))

is surjective modulo torsion,
Conjecture
1.2
is true for the generic fiber
X
.

Remark 6.3
.

The cited references only contain a proof of the first statement. But the second statement follows from the same proof. The general result of Saito produces a cohomology class
\xi\in H^{2d}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
whose restriction to each local place coincide with the class of
z_{\mu}
(
[
CT99
, Proposition 3.1]
). The various types of integral Tate conjecture are simply used to find a global cycle whose class agrees with
\xi
in various cohomology groups. See also Page 19 of the slide of Colliot-Thélène’s lecture at Cambridge in 2008 (available at
https://www.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/~jean-louis.colliot-thelene/expocambridge240809.pdf
).

We mention another closely related question.

Question 6.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety defined over a henselian local field with finite residue field. Is the cycle class map

CH_{0}(X)\hat{\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{2d}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))

injective? Here
\ell
is a prime number invertible in the residue field.

One should expect a negative answer in general. But the author is not aware of an explicit example in the literature.

Remark 6.5
.

Question
6.4
has a positive answer if
X
is a geometrically rational surface, and has a regular model with SNC central fiber (
[
EW16
, Theorem 3.1]
in general and
[
Sai91
, Theorem A]
for the case of
p
-adic fields without requiring having SNC central fiber).
In this case, the proof in
[
EW16
]
also shows that the closed fiber also satisfies a version of the integral Tate conjecture.
For
X
defined over a Laurent field
\mathbb{F}_{q}\left(\hskip-2.33336pt\hbox{$\left(\hbox{$t$}\right)$}\hskip-2.33336pt\right)
, a regular model with SNC central fiber always exists since we have resolution of singularities for
3
-folds.

If Question
6.4
has a positive answer for the generic fiber
X
, then Conjectures
1.1
and
1.3
are equivalent for
X
.

6.2.
Degree 3 unramified cohomology

By Lefschetz hyperplane theorem, one reduces the integral Tate conjecture for one-cycles for all varieties to the same conjecture for threefolds. In this case, the conjecture is about codimension
2
cycles, and thus closely related to the degree
3
unramified cohomology by the work of
[
CTK13
]
.

We summarize the relevant points. Denote by
\pi:X_{\text{\'{e}t}}\to X_{\text{Zar}}
the morphism from the étale site to the Zariski site of
X
. For a prime number
\ell
relatively prime to the characteristic of the base field, and an integer
n
, define
\mathcal{H}^{i}(\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes j})=R^{i}\pi_{*}\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes j}
.

We define the degree
i
unramified cohomology as
H^{i}_{\text{nr}}(X,\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes j})=H^{0}(X,\mathcal{H}^{i}(\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes j}))
and
H^{i}_{\text{nr}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(j))
as the inductive limit
\lim\limits_{\xrightarrow[n]{}}H^{0}(X,\mathcal{H}^{i}(\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes j}))
.

Theorem 6.6

(
[
CTK13
, Théorème 2.2, Proposition 3.2]
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective geometrically irreducible variety defined over a field
k
.

(1)

Assume that
k
has finite
\ell
-cohomological dimension. Let
M
be the cokernel of the cycle class map
\text{CH}^{2}(X)\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{4}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2))
. Then the torsion part of
M
is isomorphic to the quotient of
H^{3}_{\text{nr}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2))
by its maximal divisible subgroup.

(2)

Assume that
k
is finite or separably closed. If the Chow group of zero-cycles with rational coefficients is universally supported in a surface, then the group
H^{3}_{\text{nr}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2))
is finite.

One should also note that the Tate conjecture for codimension
2
cycles would imply that the cokernel
M
as above has to be torsion.

In
[
CTK13
]
, the authors deduced a short exact sequence relating various Chow groups of codimension
2
cycles and degree
3
unramified cohomology.
Their short exact sequence for varieties over finite fields reads the following (
[
CTK13
, Théorème 6.8]
):

\displaystyle 0\to

\displaystyle\text{Ker}(\text{CH}^{2}(X)\to\text{CH}^{2}(\overline{X}))\to H^{1}(\mathbb{F},\oplus_{\ell}H^{3}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2))_{\text{tors}})

(16)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\text{Ker}(H^{3}_{\text{nr}}(X,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(2))\to H^{3}_{\text{nr}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(2)))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\text{Coker}(\text{CH}^{2}(X)\to\text{CH}^{2}(\overline{X})^{G})\to 0

There is a similar exact sequence for
\ell
-primary torsions (
[
CTK13
]
proved this result by first proving the exact sequence for each prime
\ell
separately).

6.3.
Proof of main theorems

Proof of Theorem
1.11
.

Recall that
G=\text{Gal}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})
is the absolute Galois group.
By Theorem
1.15
(
[
KT23
, Theorem 7]
), we have an isomorphism

A_{1}(\mathcal{X})\cong A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})^{G}.

Under the assumptions (A), (B) of Theorem
1.11
, we know that there is an isomorphism of
\text{Gal}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})
-modules:

A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\cong H^{2d}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)).

Note that
G
is generated by the Frobenius
F
and
A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})^{G}
is the kernel of
F^{*}-\text{id}
. Since
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
is a flat
\mathbb{Z}
-module, we have
A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})^{G}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
is the kernel of

(F^{*}-\text{id})\otimes\text{id}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}:A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}.

That is,

A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})^{G}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\cong(A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})^{G}\cong H^{2d}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))^{G},

where
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
is equipped with the trivial action of
G
.

This proves part (
1
) of the theorem. Part (
2
) of the theorem is just Corollary
5.27
.
∎

For an algebraic subvariety
Z
of dimension
d
in a variety
X
, we have its fundamental class
\eta_{Z}\in H_{2d}^{\text{BM}}(Z,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\cong\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
.
See the last line of
[
BO74
, Example 2.1]
for the case of
\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{m}
-coefficient. The
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-coefficient case follows by taking inverse limit.
The
\ell
-adic cycle class map is defined by
Z\mapsto i_{*}(\eta_{Z})
, where
i_{*}:H_{2d}^{\text{BM}}(Z,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\to H_{2d}^{\text{BM}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
is the push-forward of Borel-Moore homology via
i:Z\to X
.

We will use the following.

Lemma 6.7

(
[
EW16
, First paragraph of Section 4.3]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}\to\text{Spec }R
be a regular scheme of relative dimension
2
over a henselian DVR with separably closed residue field, such that the central fiber is a simple normal crossing divisor.
Denote by
A
the reduced closed subscheme of the central fiber. Assume that the geometric generic fiber is rational.
The
\ell
-adic cycle class map

\text{CH}_{1}(A){\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(A,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1)).

is surjective.

Proof.

This follows from computations in
[
EW16
]
, discussed in the first paragraph of Section 4.3 of the paper.

More precisely, in the course of the proving Theorems 3.1 and 4.1 of
[
EW16
]
, Esnault and Wittenberg have shown that, under the hypothesis of the lemma, certain complex ((4.6) op. cit.) is injective, which, by an argument similar to Section 2.2 op. cit., turns out to be equivalent to the surjectivity of the cycle class map ((4.7) op. cit.):

\text{CH}_{1}(A)\hat{\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{4}_{A}(\mathfrak{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2)).

Denote by
\{A_{i},i\in I\}
the set of reduced irreducible components of
A
. Each
A_{i}
is a smooth projective surface.
Thus
A_{1}(A_{i})
is finitely generated (Theorem of the base
[
SGA71
, XIII Théorème 5.1]
).
Therefore the natural map

\text{CH}_{1}(A_{i})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to A_{1}(A_{i})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\cong A_{1}(A_{i})\hat{\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\cong\text{CH}_{1}(A_{i})\hat{\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}

is surjective, where the last equality holds because
\text{CH}_{1}(A_{i})_{\text{alg}}
is divisible.
We also have a surjection
\oplus\text{CH}_{1}(A_{i})/\ell^{m}\to\text{CH}_{1}(A)/\ell^{m}
. Since
\oplus\text{CH}_{1}(A_{i})/\ell^{m}
is a finite group, the Mittag-Leffler condition is satisfied and we have a surjection
\oplus\text{CH}_{1}(A_{i})\hat{\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to\text{CH}_{1}(A)\hat{\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
after taking the inverse limit.
Therefore the
\ell
-adic cycle class map

\oplus\text{CH}_{1}(A_{i})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to\text{CH}_{1}(A)\hat{\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{4}_{A}(\mathfrak{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2))

is surjective.
Since the above map factors through
\text{CH}_{1}(A){\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
, the
\ell
-adic cycle class map

\text{CH}_{1}(A){\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{4}_{A}(\mathfrak{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2)).

is surjective.

We have a canonical isomorphism

H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(A,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{m}(1))\cong H^{4}_{A}(\mathfrak{X},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{m}(2)).

To prove this isomorphism, consider the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}A@>{\iota}>{}>\mathfrak{X}\\
@V{}V{g}V@V{}V{f}V\\
\text{Spec }\kappa @>{h}>{}>\text{Spec }R\end{CD}

For
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{m}
,
we have
g^{!}\Lambda=\iota^{!}\Lambda(d+1)[2d+2]
(where every functor is derived) in the derived category of étale sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules
[
EW16
, (2.6)]
, where
d=2
is the relative dimension of
\mathfrak{X}\to\text{Spec }R
. Since
\iota^{!}
is the same as the derived functor of
\Gamma_{A}
, the global section with support functor (
[
SGA73
, XVIII 3.1.8]
), we have this isomorphism.
Taking inverse limit, we have an isomorphism

H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(A,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))\cong H^{4}_{A}(\mathfrak{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2)).

Moreover, this isomorphism is compatible with the cycle class map from
\text{CH}_{1}(A)\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
by the commutativity of the following diagram, where
Z\subset A
is a closed subvariety of dimension one.

\begin{CD}H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(Z,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))@>{}>{}>H^{4}_{Z}(\mathfrak{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2))\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(A,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))@>{}>{}>H^{4}_{A}(\mathfrak{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2)).\end{CD}

∎

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
\mathcal{X}^{0}\to B^{0}
be a smooth projective family of relative dimension
d
of separably rationally connected varieties over a smooth
affine
curve
B^{0}
defined over an algebraically closed field.
Then the étale cohomology group
H^{2d}(\mathcal{X}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
is generated by the class of a section.

Proof.

We compute
H^{2d}({\mathcal{X}}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
via the Leray spectral sequence.
Since the base
B^{0}
is an affine curve, the
E_{2}
-page has only two columns:
H^{0}(B^{0},\mathcal{H}^{q}),H^{1}(B^{0},\mathcal{H}^{q})
, where
\mathcal{H}^{q}
is the
\ell
-adic local system coming from degree
q

\ell
-adic cohomology of the fibers (
q=0,\ldots,2d
), and thus the spectral sequence degenerates at
E_{2}
.
Moreover, since the smooth fibers have no
H^{2d-1}
since smooth projective SRC varieties are simply connected, we have an isomorphism

H^{2d}({\mathcal{X}}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{0}(B^{0},\mathcal{H}^{2d})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}.

We have a section of
{\mathcal{X}}^{0}\to{B}^{0}
by the main theorem in
[
dJS03
]
.
The cycle class of a section generates the group
H^{0}(B^{0},\mathcal{H}^{2d})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.7
.

We first show that hypotheses (A)-(D) in Theorem
1.11
are satisfied for the smooth projective model
\mathcal{X}
.

For hypothesis (A), note that the surjectivity of the cycle class map for one-cycles is a birational invariant (Lemma
2.36
).
So using resolution of singularities for
3
-folds
[
CP08
,
CP09
,
Abh98
]
, we may assume that the singular fibers are SNC divisors.

We take
\cup\mathcal{X}_{i}
to be a non-empty finite union of fibers that contains all the singular fibers of the fibration
\mathcal{X}\to B
and
\mathcal{X}^{0}
its complement.
We have a commutative diagram of localization exact sequences, where the horizontal maps are localization sequences for Chow groups
[
Ful98
, Proposition 1.8]
(resp. étale Borel-Moore homology) applied to the triple
(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\cup\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{i,\text{red}},\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{0})
(where
\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{i,\text{red}}
means the reduced closed subscheme of
\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{i}
), and the vertical maps are cycle class maps for Chow groups:

(17)

\begin{CD}\oplus\text{CH}_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{i,\text{red}})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}@>{}>{}>\text{CH}_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}@>{}>{}>\text{CH}_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{0})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\oplus H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{i,\text{red}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))@>{}>{}>H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))@>{}>{}>H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))\\
\end{CD}

The commutativity of the left square follows from functoriality of Borel-Moore homology under proper push-forward. The commutativity of the right square follows from the fact that fundamental class commutes with restriction to an open subset (See
[
BO74
, 1.3.4, and Example 2.1]
for the case of
\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{m}
-coefficient. The
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-coefficient case follows by taking inverse limit).

We apply Lemma
6.7
in our situation.
For each fiber
\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{i}
over a point
b_{i}\in B(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, we base change
\overline{\mathcal{X}}\to\bar{B}
to
{\mathfrak{X}}_{i}\to\text{Spec }\mathcal{O}_{\bar{B},b_{i}}^{\text{h}}
, where
\mathcal{O}_{B,b_{i}}^{\text{h}}
is the henselization of the local ring
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{B},b_{i}}
at
b_{i}
. Still denote by
\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{i}
the closed fiber of
\mathfrak{X}_{i}
.
The
\ell
-adic cycle class map

\text{CH}_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{i,\text{red}})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{i,\text{red}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))

is surjective by Lemma
6.7
.

Since we assume that the union
\cup\mathcal{X}_{i}
is non-empty, the open complement
\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{0}
is over an affine curve
B^{0}\subset B
.
By Lemma
6.8
,
the cycle class of a section generates the group
H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))\cong H^{0}(B^{0},\mathcal{H}^{4})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
, and
thus the right vertical cycle class map in the commutative diagram (
17
) is surjective.
So the middle map in the commutative diagram (
17
) is also surjective.
This proves that hypothesis (A) in Theorem
1.11
is satisfied.

The result of Bloch-Srinivas
[
BS83
, Theorem 1, (iii)]
shows that the Griffiths group of one-cycles on
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
is
p
-torsion (since for
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
the rational Chow group of zero-cycles is universally supported on a curve by Corollary
2.6
). So hypothesis (B) in Theorem
1.11
is satisfied.

Hypotheses (C) and (D) in Theorem
1.11
are also satisfied in this case by Lemma
5.25
.

The Hochschild-Serre spectral sequence gives a short exact sequence:

0\to H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{2d-2r-1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-r)))\to H^{2d-2r}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-r))\to H^{2d-2r}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-r))^{G}\to 0,

where
G
is the absolute Galois group of
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q}
.
Thus Theorem
1.11
implies that
\text{CH}_{1}(\mathcal{X})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{4}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}_{l}(2))
is surjective.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

This follows from Theorem
6.2
,
1.7
, and Remark
6.5
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

If there is a rational point everywhere locally and the local points satisfy the Brauer-Manin constraint, there is a zero-cycle of degree
1
over the function field by Theorem
1.4
.
A del Pezzo surface of degree
4
is a complete intersection of
2
quadrics in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
.
Such a complete intersection has a rational point if and only if it has an odd degree
0
-cycle
[
Bru78
]
.
Hence we have the result.
∎

Proof of Corollary
1.14
.

The vanishing of
H^{3}_{\text{nr}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2))
follows from Theorem
1.7
and
[
CTK13
, Théorème 2.2, Proposition 3.2]
(recalled in Theorem
6.6
), and the fact that
CH_{0}(\overline{X})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is universally supported in a curve (Corollary
2.6
).

We have already shown that the cycle class map is surjective in Theorem
1.7
. But it is also injective on torsions
[
CTSS83
, Théorème 4]
. In particular, it is injective on the algebraically trivial part, i.e.
\text{CH}_{1}(X)_{\text{alg}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{1}(\mathbb{F},H^{3}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2))
is injective. We also have an isomorphism
A_{1}(X)\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\cong A_{1}(\overline{X})^{G}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\cong H^{4}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(2))^{G}
. This proves that the cycle class map is an isomorphism.

It follows from the exact sequence (
6.2
) that we have the description of the Chow groups of
X
and
\overline{X}
.
∎

7.
Examples

We give some examples where one can check the conditions in Theorem
1.11
.
We first make a simple observation about the coniveau filtration.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\mathcal{X}^{0}\to B^{0}
be a smooth projective family of relative dimension
d
over an affine curve
B^{0}
defined over an algebraically closed field.
Fix a prime number
\ell
different from the characteristic.
Assume that a general fiber
\mathcal{X}_{b}
is separably rationally connected, and that the cycle class map induces a surjective map

A_{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{\text{BM}}_{2}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1)).

Then
N^{d-1}H^{2d-1}(\mathcal{X}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))=H^{2d-1}(\mathcal{X}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
.

Proof.

We use the Leray spectral sequence for
\mathcal{X}^{0}\to B^{0}
.
Since
B^{0}
is affine, the
E_{2}
-page has only two columns:
H^{0}(B^{0},\mathcal{H}^{q})
and
H^{1}(B^{0},\mathcal{H}^{q})
, where
\mathcal{H}^{q}
is the
\ell
-adic local system corresponding to the degree
q

\ell
-adic cohomology of the fiber, and thus the spectral sequence degenerates.
Since a general fiber is separably rationally connected, it is simply connected.
In particular, a general fiber has no
H^{2d-1}
.
So we have the isomorphism

H^{2d-1}(\mathcal{X}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\cong H^{1}(B^{0},\mathcal{H}^{2d-2}).

By assumption, for a general fiber
\mathcal{X}_{b}
,
H^{2d-2}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))
is generated by the classes of finitely many algebraic curves.
Since
\mathcal{X}_{b}
is separably rationally connected, the usual argument involving adding very free curves and smoothing shows that
H^{2d-2}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))
is generated by classes of finitely many curves that deforms to general fibers (see for example
[
KT23
, Theorem 46]
).
For each of these curves, choose a deformation
T\leftarrow\Sigma_{i}\to\mathcal{X}
which surjects onto
B^{0}
.
Then

H^{2d-1}(\mathcal{X}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\cong H^{1}(B^{0},\mathcal{H}^{2d-2})

is supported on the union of
\cup_{i}\Sigma_{i}
.
∎

Corollary 7.2
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\to B
be a flat projective family over a smooth projective curve defined over an algebraically closed field.
Assume that the generic fiber is smooth projective and separably rationally connected.
Fix a prime number
\ell
different from the characteristic.
If the following two conditions hold:

(1)

for a general fiber
\mathcal{X}_{b}
, the cycle class map induces a surjective map

A_{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{\text{BM}}_{2}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1));

(2)

for every singular fiber
\mathcal{X}_{b}
,
H_{3}^{\text{BM}}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))
is supported on a two dimensional Zariski closed subset of
\mathcal{X}_{b}
,

we have

N^{d-2}H^{2d-1}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))=H^{2d-1}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)).

Proof.

Apply the localization exact sequence to the finite union of singular fibers and its complement.
∎

To determine
A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})
seems to be a difficult problem in general.
We provide a simple example where one can use some global geometry to do so.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{P}_{B}(E)
be a family of complete intersections of degree
d_{1},\ldots,d_{c}
in
\mathbb{P}^{n}
over a smooth projective curve
B
over an algebraically closed field.
Assume that
\sum d_{i}^{2}\leq n
.

(1)

The cycle class of any two sections
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\subset\mathcal{X}
of
\mathcal{X}\to B
are rationally equivalent modulo lines in general fibers.

(2)

More generally, if
C\subset\mathcal{X}
is any irreducible curve such that
C\to\mathcal{X}\to B
has degree
d\geq 1
, then the cycle class of
C
is rationally equivalent to
d[\sigma_{0}]
for a section
\sigma_{0}
modulo lines in general fibers.

(3)

Any curve in a fiber is rationally equivalent to a sum of lines in general fibers.

(4)

The group of one-cycles modulo algebraic equivalence,
A_{1}(\mathcal{X})
, is a free abelian group of rank
2
, generated by the class of a section and a line in a general fiber.

Note that Tsen-Lang theorem implies that there are sections of
\mathcal{X}\to B
under the assumptions on degrees.

Proof.

Let
H
be the scheme parameterizing the space of chains of two lines passing through
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
, that is, curves of the form
(L_{1}\cup L_{2},x_{0},x_{1},x_{2})
, where
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are two lines in a fiber
\mathcal{X}_{b},b\in B
intersecting at a single point
x_{0}
,
x_{1}\in L_{1},x_{2}\in L_{2}
and
x_{1}=\sigma_{1}(b),x_{2}=\sigma_{2}(b)
.
The morphism
H\to B
is proper, because a degeneration of a chain of two lines is again a chain of two lines.

The space of chains of two lines passing through two points in a complete intersection of degree
d_{1},\ldots,d_{c}
in
\mathbb{P}^{n}
is defined by equations of degree
1,1,2,2,\ldots,d_{1}-1,d_{1}-1,d_{1},1,1,\ldots,d_{2}-1,d_{2}-1,d_{2},\ldots,1,1,\ldots,d_{c}-1,d_{c}-1,d_{c}
in
\mathbb{P}^{n}
(see, for example, Lemma 3.4 in
[
Pan18
]
).

The classical Tsen-Lang theorem applied to the generic fiber of
H\to B
implies that there is a rational section
B\dashrightarrow H
. Since
B
is a curve, we can extend this to a section.
This is equivalent to the following.
The family
\mathcal{X}\to B
contains two ruled surfaces over
B
:
B\leftarrow\Sigma_{1}\subset\mathcal{X},B\leftarrow\Sigma_{2}\subset\mathcal{X}
, such that
\sigma_{1}\subset\Sigma_{1}
(resp.
\sigma_{2}\subset\Sigma_{2}
) is a section.
Moreover,
\Sigma_{1}\cap\Sigma_{2}=\sigma_{0}
is a common section of both ruled surfaces. And
\Sigma_{1}\cup_{\sigma_{0}}\Sigma_{2}\subset\mathcal{X}
is a family of chains of two lines connecting
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
.

Any two sections in a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over a curve are rationally equivalent modulo general fibers.
Thus both
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
are rationally equivalent to
\sigma_{0}
up to lines in general fibers.
Pushing forward this rational equivalence to
\mathcal{X}
proves the first statement.

Given
C\subset\mathcal{X}
such that
C\to B
is dominant of degree
d
, we make a base change
\mathcal{X}_{C}=\mathcal{X}\times_{B}C\xrightarrow{(p,q)}\mathcal{X}\times C
.
The family
\mathcal{X}_{C}\to C
admits two sections:
\sigma_{C}
, induced by the inclusion
C\subset\mathcal{X}
, and
\sigma_{B}
, induced by
C\to B\to\sigma_{0}\subset\mathcal{X}
.
By previous argument,
[\sigma_{C}]
and
[\sigma_{B}]
are rationally equivalent modulo lines in general fibers.
We push forward this rational equivalence relation to
\mathcal{X}
via
p
. Since
p_{*}[\sigma_{C}]=[C],p_{*}[\sigma_{B}]=d[\sigma_{0}]
, and the push-forward of lines in general fibers are still lines in general fibers, we prove the second statement.

Any curve in a fiber is rationally equivalent to the difference of two multi-sections of the same degree.
So it is also rationally equivalent to a sum of cycle class of lines in general fibers.

Finally, since the Fano scheme of lines of a complete intersection of degree
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{c})
in
\mathbb{P}^{n}
is connected as long as
2n-c-\sum d_{i}>2

[
DM98
, Théorème 2.1]
, all lines in the fibers are algebraically equivalent.
This proves the fourth statement.
∎

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{P}_{B}(E)
be a family of complete intersections of degree
d_{1},\ldots,d_{c},d_{i}\geq 2,
in
\mathbb{P}^{n}
over a smooth projective curve
B
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.
Assume that the generic fiber
X
is smooth separably rationally connected of dimension
d=n-c
, that
\sum d_{i}^{2}\leq n
, and that the total space
\mathcal{X}
is smooth.
If one of the following conditions holds

(1)

c=1
, i.e.
X
is a smooth hypersurface;

(2)

for every geometric fiber
X_{b}
,
H^{\text{BM}}_{2}(X_{b},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
is generated by the class of a line and
H^{\text{BM}}_{3}(X_{b},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})=0
.

Then the cycle class map

\text{CH}^{d}(\mathcal{X})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{2d}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))

is surjective and Conjectures
1.1
and
1.2
hold for the generic fiber
X
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(B)
.

Remark 7.5
.

Under the assumptions, there is a rational point for the generic fiber
X
since
\mathbb{F}_{q}(B)
is
C_{2}
and normic forms exist. Thus the Hasse principle part (Conjecture
1.2
) is automatic.

Remark 7.6
.

In general it is still an open question if a smooth Fano complete intersection is separably rationally connected. However, one can show that if the characteristic
p
is larger than all the
d_{i}
, then every smooth Fano complete intersection of degree
d_{1},\ldots,d_{c}
is separably rationally connected
[
STZ22
]
.

Remark 7.7
.

The numerical conditions imply that
X
is either a quadric of dimension
3
or has dimension at least
4
.
Thus the condition
7.4
.(2) on Borel-Moore homology holds for all smooth fibers by Lefschetz hyperplane theorem.
It also holds for singular fibers with ordinary double points.

Proof of Propostion
7.4
.

By Theorem
1.11
and by Theorem
6.2
,
it suffices to show:

(1)

A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\cong H^{2d}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)).

(2)

N^{d-1}H^{2d-1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))=H^{2d-1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)).

There is an open subset
B^{0}\subset\bar{B}
such that
\mathcal{X}^{0}=\overline{\mathcal{X}}|_{B^{0}}
is a complete intersection of ample divisors in
\mathbb{P}(E)|_{B^{0}}
.
In case
7.4
.(1), we take
B^{0}
in such a way that
B\backslash B^{0}
is a single point with smooth fiber.
In case
7.4
.(2), we take
B^{0}
so that
{\mathcal{X}}^{0}\to B^{0}
is smooth.

Write
\mathcal{X}_{0}
to be the disjoint union of all the fibers over
B\backslash B^{0}
.
Denote by
\mathbb{P}^{0}
the open subscheme
\overline{\mathbb{P}_{B}(E)}|_{B^{0}}
, and
U^{0}
the open subscheme
\mathbb{P}^{0}\backslash\mathcal{X}^{0}
.

We use the localization sequence of étale cohomology

\displaystyle\ldots\to

\displaystyle H^{2d-1}_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))\to H^{2d-1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\to H^{2d-1}({\mathcal{X}}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle H^{2d}_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))\to H^{2d}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\to H^{2d}({\mathcal{X}}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle H^{2d+1}(\mathcal{X}_{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))\ldots,

We have the isomorphisms

H^{2d-3}_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))\cong H^{\text{BM}}_{3}(\mathcal{X}_{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))=0,

H^{2d-2}_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))\cong\oplus_{b\in B\backslash B^{0}}H^{\text{BM}}_{2}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1))\cong\oplus_{b\in B\backslash B^{0}}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\cdot[L_{b}],

where
[L_{b}]
is the class of a line in
\mathcal{X}_{b}
.
In the case where
X
is a hypersurface,
the above isomorphisms follow from Remark
7.7
and standard facts about cohomology of smooth hypersurfaces.
In the case of complete intersections, the above isomorphisms follow from our assumption.

Moreover the map

H^{2d-2}_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))\to H^{2d}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))

has one-dimensional image spanned by the class of a line in the total space by Lemma
7.3
, part (3) and (4).

In case
7.4
.(1), we use the following localization long exact sequence:

\displaystyle H^{2d}(U^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))\to H^{2d-1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}|_{B^{0}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{2d+1}(\mathbb{P}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))

\displaystyle H^{2d+1}(U^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))\to H^{2d}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}|_{B^{0}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{2d+2}(\mathbb{P}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle H^{2d+2}(U^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))\ldots.

The open complement
U^{0}
is an affine variety of dimension
n+1
. Under our assumptions on
d
and
n
, we have
2d=2n-2>n+1
.
So by Artin vanishing for étale cohomology,

H^{2d}(U^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))=H^{2d+1}(U^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))=H^{2d+2}(U^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))=0.

Therefore we have isomorphisms

H^{2d-1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}|_{B^{0}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{2d+1}(\mathbb{P}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1)),

H^{2d}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}|_{B^{0}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{2d+2}(\mathbb{P}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1)).

We know that
H^{2d}(\mathbb{P}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))
is spanned by the class of a section by Lemma
6.8
, and that
H^{2d-1}(\mathbb{P}^{0},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))
is supported on a surface swept out by a family of lines by Lemma
7.1
.
Since we also have a section of
\overline{\mathcal{X}}\to B
and we may choose the family of lines to lie in
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, the same is true for
\mathcal{X}^{0}=\overline{\mathcal{X}}|_{B^{0}}
in this case.

In case
7.4
.(2), the group
H^{2d}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}|_{B^{0}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
is spanned by the class of a section by Lemma
6.8
.
So
H^{2d}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
is spanned by the class of a section and a line.
By Corollary
7.2
, our assumption on singular fibers also implies that
H^{2d-1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
is supported on a surface.

Over the algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, by Lemma
7.3
, the group
A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})
is a free abelian group of rank
2
, generated by the class of a section and a line in a general fiber.
As a result,

A_{1}(\overline{\mathcal{X}})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\cong H^{2d}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)).

This concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.8
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(B)
.
Assume that

(1)

X
admits a regular projective model
\mathcal{X}\to B
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.

(2)

The base change of
X
to
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(B)
becomes stably rational (over
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(B)
).

Then the cycle class map

\text{CH}^{d}(\mathcal{X})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{2d}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))

is surjective (where
d
is the dimension of
X
) and Conjecture
1.2
holds for
X
.

Proof.

The validity of the hypotheses in Theorem
1.11
only depends on the stable birational class of the generic fiber over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(B)
by Lemma
2.36
. They are clearly true if the generic fiber over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(B)
is
\mathbb{P}^{n}
.
∎

Remark 7.9
.

Certain moduli spaces of stable vector bundles on curves defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(B)
become rational/stably rational over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(B)
(e.g. if the curve has a rational point over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(B)

[
Hof07
, Corollary 6.2]
).
Unfortunately, it is not known if there exists a regular model for such moduli spaces over
B
in the general case.

8.
Questions and expectations

8.1.
Validity of hypotheses in Theorem
1.11

The main questions left open from the current work is the validity of the hypotheses (A)-(D) in Theorem
1.11
.

A result of Schoen
[
Sch98
]
says that if the Tate conjecture is true for divisors on all smooth projective surfaces defined over finite fields, then for any smooth projective variety
V
defined over a finite field
\mathbb{F}
, the cycle class map

\text{CH}_{1}(\bar{V})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{l}\to\cup_{K/\mathbb{F}}H^{2d-2}(\bar{V},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))^{\text{Gal}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}/K)}(\subset H^{2d-2}(\bar{V},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1)))

is surjective, where
\bar{V}
is the base change of
V
to an algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbb{F}}
of
\mathbb{F}
, and the union is taken over all finite extensions
\mathbb{F}\subset K\subset\overline{\mathbb{F}}
.

If furthermore
V
is SRC in codimension one, since its rational Chow group of zero-cycles is universally supported in a curve (Corollary
2.6
), it is easy to see that every class in
H^{2d-2}(\bar{V},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(d-1))
is algebraic. Thus every class in
H^{2d-2}(\bar{V},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1))
is fixed by some open subgroup of the Galois group.
So in this case, Schoen’s theorem implies that we always have a surjection

\text{CH}_{1}(\bar{V})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{2d-2}(\bar{V},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1)),

provided that the Tate conjecture holds for all surfaces.

The author has made conjectures on the Kato homology of rationally connected fibrations over an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
in
[
Tia20
, Conjecture 1.16]
. A special case of the conjectures in loc. cit. is the following:

Conjecture 8.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety defined over an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
. Assume that
X
is separably rationally connected in codimension one. Then the cycle class map on higher Chow groups induces an isomorphism

\text{CH}_{1}(X,i,\mathbb{Z}/N)\cong H_{2+i}(X,\mathbb{Z}/N)

for all
i
, where the homology group is the Borel-Moore homology.

This conjecture implies that for a smooth projective variety that is separably rationally connected in codimension one defined over an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
, the statements of hypotheses (A), (B), (C), and (D) hold. It is quite reasonable to believe that the same is true in characteristic
p>0
with
N
relatively prime to
p
.

Over the complex numbers, Conjecture
8.1
can be formulated in terms of Lawson homology with
\mathbb{Z}
-coefficients. Using the decomposition of the diagonal argument (See the proof of Proposition 5.2, 5.3, 5.4, 5.5 in
[
Voi08
]
for examples of the argument in dimension
3
and
4
), one can show that when
X
is SRC in codimension one, for each
i
, the kernel and cokernel of s-maps
L_{1}H_{i}(X)\to H_{i}(X)
are
N
-torsion.
We have a commutative diagram of long exact sequences

\begin{CD}L_{1}H_{i}(X,\mathbb{Z})@>{\cdot N}>{}>L_{1}H_{i}(X,\mathbb{Z})@>{}>{}>L_{1}H_{i}(X,\mathbb{Z}/N)@>{}>{}>L_{1}H_{i-1}(X,\mathbb{Z})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
H_{i}(X,\mathbb{Z})@>{\cdot N}>{}>H_{i}(X,\mathbb{Z})@>{}>{}>H_{i}(X,\mathbb{Z}/N)@>{}>{}>H_{i-1}(X,\mathbb{Z}).\\
\end{CD}

By results of Suslin-Voevodsky
[
SV96
, Theorem 9.1]
and the Beilinson-Lichtenbaum conjecture proved by Voevodsky (Theorem
2.8
),
there is an isomorphism

L_{1}H_{2+i}(X,\mathbb{Z}/N)\xrightarrow{\cong}\text{CH}_{1}(X,i,\mathbb{Z}/N)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,\tau^{\leq\dim X-1}R\pi_{*}(\mathbb{Z}/N))

between torsion Lawson homology, Bloch’s higher Chow group, and certain Zariski cohomology group, where
\pi:X_{cl}\to X_{zar}
is the continuous map from
X(\mathbb{C})
with the analytic topology to
X
with the Zariski topology (See Corollary
2.12
for the last isomorphism).

Then Conjecture
8.1
is equivalent to the following.

Conjecture 8.2
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective complex variety that is SRC in codimension one. The s-maps

s:L_{1}H_{i}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to H_{i}(X,\mathbb{Z})

are isomorphisms for all
i
.

8.2.
Injectivity of cycle class map

Even though the author believes that Question
6.4
has a negative answer in general, when we restrict ourselves to separably rationally connected varieties, there might be a chance of having a positive answer.

Question 8.3
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective separably rationally connected variety defined over a henselian local field with finite or separably closed residue field. Is the cycle class map

CH_{0}(X)\hat{\otimes}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\to H^{2d}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))

injective? Here
\ell
is a prime number invertible in the residue field.

By the work of Saito-Sato
[
SS10
, Theorem 1.16]
, if
X
admits a regular model
\mathfrak{X}\to\text{Spec }R
such that the cental fiber is a simple normal crossing (SNC) divisor, the cycle class map induces an isomorphism

\text{CH}^{d}(\mathfrak{X})/\ell^{n}\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{2d}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\mathfrak{X},\mu_{\ell^{n}}),

where
d
the the dimension of
X
.
We have the following commutative diagram of localization sequences for Chow groups and étale cohomology:

\begin{CD}\text{CH}^{d-1}(\mathfrak{X}_{0})/\ell^{n}@>{}>{}>\text{CH}^{d}(\mathfrak{X})/\ell^{n}@>{}>{}>\text{CH}^{d}(X)/\ell^{n}\to 0\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{\cong}V@V{}V{}V\\
H^{2d}_{\mathfrak{X}_{0}}(\mathfrak{X},\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes d})@>{}>{}>H^{2d}(\mathfrak{X},\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes d})@>{}>{}>H^{2d}(X,\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes d}),\end{CD}

where the left vertical map maybe identified with the cycle class map to the Borel-Moore homology

\text{CH}_{1}(\mathfrak{X}_{0})/\ell^{n}\to H_{2}^{\text{BM}}(\mathfrak{X}_{0},\mu_{\ell^{n}}).

By a simple diagram chasing, we would have a positive answer to Question
8.3
if this cycle class map were surjective (i.e. the central fiber
\mathfrak{X}_{0}
satisfies the integral Tate conjecture for one-cycles).

The author studied this question for rationally connected varieties defined over the Laurent field over
\mathbb{C}
in
[
Tia20
]
. The main technical result in
[
Tia20
]
is a dévissage statement, a special case of which implies that the surjectivity of cycle class maps of one-cycles for SNC degenerations of rationally connected varieties over Laurent fields would follow from the corresponding surjectivity for smooth projective rationally connected varieties. The proof of this dévissage only depends on properties of the minimal model program. Since the minimal model program is expected to hold in positive and mixed characteristic as well, we may expect that such a dévissage type result is also true in positive and mixed characteristic (but in positive and mixed characteristic, one has to be careful about what should be the right class of varieties, since rational connectedness is not as well-behaved). Then combined with the expectations from previous subsection (and Theorem
1.11
), we may expect a positive answer to Question
8.3
when
X
is separably rationally connected.
